26-00-00-040-813MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Preparation - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-813

Task Description

2. MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Preparation - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers.

B. References

Reference Title 26-26-01-020-801 Halon Fire Extinguisher Removal (P/B 401) 26-26-02-000-801 Water Fire Extinguisher Removal (P/B 401)

C. Deactivation of Inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers

SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-008 (1) Do the applicable task to remove the inoperative portable fire extinguisher: Halon Fire Extinguisher Removal, TASK 26-26-01-020-801 or Water Fire Extinguisher Removal, TASK 26-26-02-000-801. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-032 (2) Stow the extinguisher. (a) Put the extinguisher in a bag (b) Make sure that the extinguisher can not be used by mistake NOTE: The extinguisher will be removed from the airplane at the next available maintenance base. SUBTASK 26-00-00-610-001 (3) Make sure that the necessary supply of serviceable extinguishers is available during maintenance.

26-00-00-440-812MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Restoration - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 440-812

Task Description

3. MMEL 26–3 (DDPG) Restoration - Portable Fire Extinguishers Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers.

B. References

Reference Title 26-26-01-430-801 Halon Fire Extinguisher Installation (P/B 401) 26-26-02-400-801 Water Fire Extinguisher Installation (P/B 401)

C. Reactivation of the Inoperative Portable Fire Extinguishers

SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-009 (1) Remove the inoperative extinguisher from the airplane. SUBTASK 26-00-00-420-006 (2) Do the applicable task to install the new portable fire extinguisher Halon Fire Extinguisher Installation, TASK 26-26-01-430-801 or Water Fire Extinguisher Installation, TASK 26-26-02-400-801.

26-00-00-040-802MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Preparation - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-802

Task Description

4. MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Preparation - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps that prepare the airplane for flight with the Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative.

B. References

Reference Title 22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401) 22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401) 26-18-00-710-801 Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection System - Operational Test (P/B 501)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left 134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Wheel Well Fire Detection System Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-210-001 (1) Just prior to engine start, check that brakes are cool per accepted procedure. SUBTASK 26-00-00-740-001 (2) Deactivate the wheel well fire detection system, use one of the two methods. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-034 (3) Method 1: deactivate WHEEL WELL light and wheel well fire detection system input to fire warning system. (a) Remove the two light bulbs from the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel. 1) Install an INOP - FIRE BELL DISCONNECTED placard on the light switch. (b) Disconnect the fire bell, do these steps: 1) Remove Flight Control Computer (FCC) B to get access to the wiring for the fire bell (Flight Control Computer Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801). 2) Disconnect the wires from D742, pin 16 and pin 25 on the E1-4 shelf. 3) Cap and stow the wires. 4) Install the FCC B (Flight Control Computer Installation, TASK 22-11-33-400-801). (c) Install a WHEEL WELL WARNING LIGHT AND BELL INOP placard on the captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights, on the P7 panel. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-035 (4) Method 2: deactivate the detection elements of the wheel well fire detection system. (a) Disconnect the terminal lugs A and B or connectors on both sides of the M270 detection elements. (b) Do this task: Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection System - Operational Test, TASK 26-18-00-710-801. 1) Make sure that all tests pass except for the wheel well fire function with the disabled element. 26-00-00 (c) Stow the wiring and terminal lugs on either side of M270 detection elements.

26-00-00-440-802MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Restoration - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 440-802

Task Description

5. MMEL 26-4 (DDPG) Restoration - Wheel Well Fire Detection System Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Wheel Well Fire Detection Systems Inoperative.

B. References

Reference Title 22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401) 22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left 134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Wheel Well Fire Detection Systems Activation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-810-002 (1) Correct the fault. (a) Find the fault code or description of the fault that occurred. (b) Go to the applicable index or list in the FIM and find the FIM task number. (c) Go to the task in the FIM and do the steps in the task. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-004 (2) Install the two light bulbs in the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel. (a) Remove an INOP - FIRE BELL DISCONNECTED placard from the switch. SUBTASK 26-00-00-010-008 (3) Remove Flight Control Computer - B to get access to the wiring for the fire bell (Flight Control Computer Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801). SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-004 (4) Install the wires from D742, pin 16 and pin 25 on the E1-4 shelf. (a) Remove the WHEEL WELL WARNING LIGHT AND BELL INOP placard from the captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights. SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-007 (5) Install Flight Control Computer - B (Flight Control Computer Installation, TASK 22-11-33-400-801). SUBTASK 26-00-00-420-007 (6) If it is necessary, install the terminal lugs A and B or connectors on both sides of the M270 detection elements.

26-00-00-040-803MMEL 26-9 (DDPG) Preparation - Engine/APU Fire Extinguisher Test System (EXT TEST) (Squib ATA 26 · Task 040-803

Task Description

6. MMEL 26-9 (DDPG) Preparation - Engine/APU Fire Extinguisher Test System (EXT TEST) (Squib Test) Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the engine/APU squib test (EXT TEST switch) inoperative.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Engine/APU Extinguisher System (EXT TEST) (Squib Test) Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-760-001 WARNING USE THE CORRECT LAMP WHEN YOU MAKE THE SQUIB TESTER. IF YOU USE AN INCORRECT LAMP , THE HIGH ELECTRICAL CURRENT CAN CAUSE THE SQUIB TO FIRE. THIS WILL RELEASE THE EXTINGUISHING AGENT . INJURIES TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. (1) Prepare to do a test of the squib circuits: (a) Make a squib test lamp with pigtail leads that end in pin contacts. Use only a 28 volt, 40 milliamp lamp in a applicable holder. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-036 (2) For the engines: (a) Open these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L (b) Pull the engine 1 and engine 2 fire handles, on the P8-1 panel. WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU OPEN THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL. THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL CONTAINS HIGH VOLTAGES AND CURRENTS THAT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (c) Loosen the screws to lower the P6-2 circuit breaker panel. WARNING MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL ARE CLEAR OF THE ENGINES AND APU. ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS. (d) Connect the test lamp across the circuit breaker shown in the table below. (e) Turn the applicable fire handle in the direction that is shown in the table. 1) Make sure that the lamp comes on for each position. NOTE: If the lamp comes on, the squib is satisfactory. 26-00-00 Table 901/26-00-00-993-801 CIRCUIT BREAKER FIRE HANDLE DIRECTION C296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT ENG 1 ENG 2 LEFT LEFT C297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT ENG 1 ENG 2 RIGHT RIGHT C1021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN LEFT ENG 1 ENG 2 LEFT LEFT C1022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN RIGHT ENG 1 ENG 2 RIGHT RIGHT (f) Remove the test lamp after you do the check for all of the circuit breakers. (g) Push the engine 1 and engine 2 fire handles, on the P8-1 panel, to the normal position. (h) Close the P6-2 panel and install the screws. (i) Close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L (3) For the APU: (a) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU (b) Pull the APU fire handles on the P8-1 panel. WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU OPEN THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL. THE P6-2 CIRCUIT BREAKER PANEL CONTAINS HIGH VOLTAGES AND CURRENTS THAT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (c) Loosen the screws to lower the P6-2 circuit breaker panel. WARNING MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL ARE CLEAR OF THE ENGINES AND APU. ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS. (d) Connect the test lamp across the circuit breaker shown in the table below. (e) Turn the applicable fire handle in the direction that is shown in the table. 1) Make sure that the lamp comes on for each position. NOTE: If the lamp comes on, the squib is satisfactory. 26-00-00 Table 902/26-00-00-993-802 CIRCUIT BREAKER FIRE HANDLE DIRECTION C452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU APU APU LEFT RIGHT (f) Remove the test lamp after you do the check for the circuit breaker. (g) Push the APU fire handles, on the P8-1 panel, to the normal position. (h) Close the P6-2 panel and install the screws. (i) Close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-005 (4) Install a TEST INOP placard on the EXT TEST switch, on the P8-1 panel.

26-00-00-040-805MMEL 26-15 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Fire Extinguisher System Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-805

Task Description

8. MMEL 26-15 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Fire Extinguisher System Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory Fire Extinguisher System inoperative.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 200 Upper Half of Fuselage

C. Lavatory Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-006 (1) If the Lavatory Smoke Detector operates, do this step. (a) Install INOP placard on the fire extinguisher bottle. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-007 (2) If the Lavatory Smoke Detector does not operate, do these steps. (a) Empty the lavatory waste receptacle. (b) Close and lock the lavatory door. (c) Install an INOPERATIVE - DO NOT ENTER placard on the lavatory door.

26-00-00-040-806MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector System Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-806

Task Description

10. MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector System Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory Smoke Detector System inoperative. 26-00-00

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 200 Upper Half of Fuselage

C. Lavatory Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-010 (1) Do the following steps: (a) Empty the lavatory waste receptacle. (b) If necessary, disconnect the smoke detector. 1) Open the applicable LAVATORY SMOKE Circuit Breaker (CB). a) Lock with a DO NOT CLOSE tag. 2) Remove the smoke detector. 3) Disconnect, cap and stow the electrical connector from the smoke detector. 4) Install the smoke detector. 5) Close the applicable LAVATORY SMOKE CB. (c) Close and lock the lavatory door. (d) Install an INOPERATIVE - DO NOT ENTER placard on the lavatory door.

26-00-00-040-814MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector Test Switch Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-814

Task Description

12. MMEL 26-16 (DDPG) Preparation - Lavatory Smoke Detector Test Switch Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lavatory Smoke Detector Test Switch inoperative. NOTE: This procedure is applicable to the inoperable SELF TEST switch, on the smoke detector, or the TEST switch, on the Flight Attendant's Panel.

B. References

Reference Title 26-14-00-730-801 Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (P/B 501) 26-14-00-730-802 Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (P/B 501)

C. Lavatory Smoke Detector Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-033 (1) For each smoke detector, do the lavatory smoke detection system test (Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test, TASK 26-14-00-730-801 or Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test, TASK 26-14-00-730-802). (a) The test switch may be inoperative provided that each lavatory smoke detector is verified to pass the smoke test.

26-00-00-040-807MMEL 26-18 (DDPG) Preparation - Wing Body Overheat Test System Inoperative ATA 26 · Task 040-807

Task Description

14. MMEL 26-18 (DDPG) Preparation - Wing Body Overheat Test System Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Wing Body Overheat Test System inoperative.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 131 Center Section Wing Box, Body Station 540.00 to Body Station 663.75 - Left 26-00-00 (Continued) Zone Area 132 Center Section Wing Box, Body Station 540.00 to Body Station 663.75 - Right 133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left 134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Check the Wing-Body Overheat Test System

SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-012 (1) Apply heat from an appropriate heat source to an operable detector element in the left wing-body overheat detection system and in the right wing-body overheat detection system. NOTE: The substitute test heat device must not produce heat greater than 450°F (232°C). This temperature limit should be observed in event fuel vapors exist in the area. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-028 (2) When a detector element in the left system is heated, observe that these lights come on: (a) Left WING-BODY OVERHEAT light on the Air Conditioning Panel (P5). (b) The AIR COND light on the glareshield P7. (c) The MASTER CAUTION lights. SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-029 (3) When a detector element in the right system is heated, observe that these lights come on: (a) Right WING-BODY OVERHEAT light on the Air Conditioning Panel (P5). (b) The AIR COND light on the glareshield P7. (c) The MASTER CAUTION lights.

26-00-00-040-808MMEL 26-19-02 (DDPG) Preparation - Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 ATA 26 · Task 040-808

Task Description

16. MMEL 26-19-02 (DDPG) Preparation - Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps that prepare the airplane for flight with the Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Inoperative.

B. References

Reference Title 20-40-12-000-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal (P/B 201) 20-40-12-400-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left 122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right 125 Air Conditioning Distribution Bay - Left 126 Air Conditioning Distribution Bay - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door

E. Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-860-001 (1) Make sure that this circuit breaker is open and has safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 SUBTASK 26-00-00-010-002 (2) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door (a) Remove the lining at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment to get access to the air conditioning distribution bay. 26-00-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued) SUBTASK 26-00-00-040-024 (3) Do the steps that follow to deactivate Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 and prevent anomalies in the indication of the DISCH light during operation and the EXT FWD and EXT AFT lights during the system test: NOTE: The steps that follow are only applicable to the Boeing System. For systems installed by STC, see the STC holder. SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-001 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIB. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (4) Disconnect the electrical connectors from the bottle. (a) Before you touch the squibs, do the procedure for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic discharge, these are the tasks: • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802 • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802. (b) Disconnect, cap, and stow electrical connector D12816 on the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 pressure switch. (c) Disconnect electrical connectors D12820 and D12818 from the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 squibs. WARNING PUT A PROTECTIVE COVER ON THE BOTTLE SQUIB. IF YOU DO NOT PUT A PROTECTIVE COVER ON THE SQUIB, THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS. (d) Install a jumper across pins 1 and 2 on the electrical connectors D12820 and D12818. (e) Cap and stow the connectors. SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-002 (5) Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door (a) Install the lining at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment (at the air conditioning distribution bay). SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-013 (6) Do the step that follow to INOP the Fire Extinguisher Bottle No. 2: (a) Install the Fire Bottle No. 2 INOP Placard on the Cargo Fire Panel (P8). SUBTASK 26-00-00-860-002 (7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2

26-00-00-440-806MMEL 26-19-02 (DDPG) Restoration - Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 ATA 26 · Task 440-806

Task Description

17. MMEL 26-19-02 (DDPG) Restoration - Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Inoperative.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left 122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right 125 Air Conditioning Distribution Bay - Left 126 Air Conditioning Distribution Bay - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door

D. Lower Cargo Compartment Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 Activation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-860-003 (1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 SUBTASK 26-00-00-010-003 (2) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door (3) Remove the lining at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment to get access to the air conditioning distribution bay. SUBTASK 26-00-00-800-001 (4) Do the steps that follow to Activate the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 System: (a) Remove the cap, and stowed electrical connector D12816, D12820 and D12818. (b) Remove the jumper across pins 1 and 2 on the connectors D12820 and D12818. (c) Remove the protective cap installed on the Bottle Squib. SUBTASK 26-00-00-420-004 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIB. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . 26-00-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued) (WARNING PRECEDES) WARNING MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN RELEASE THE CONTENTS OF THE BOTTLE SUDDENLY . THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL. (5) Connect the electrical connectors to the Extinguisher Bottle. (a) Connect the electrical connector D12816 on the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 pressure switch. (b) Connect electrical connectors D12820 and D12818 on the Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 squibs SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-003 (6) Install the liner at the aft end of the forward cargo compartment to the air conditioning distribution bay. Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 821 Forward Cargo Door SUBTASK 26-00-00-930-018 (7) Do the step that follow to Activate the Fire Extinguisher No. 2 : (a) Remove the Fire Extinguisher Bottle No. 2 INOP Placard on the Cargo Fire Panel. SUBTASK 26-00-00-860-004 (8) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 RYR ALL

26-00-00-040-809MMEL 26-19-04/-06 (DDPG) Preparation - Lower Cargo Compartment DISCH or EXT Light ATA 26 · Task 040-809

Task Description

18. MMEL 26-19-04/-06 (DDPG) Preparation - Lower Cargo Compartment DISCH or EXT Light Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Lower Cargo Compartment DISCH or EXT Light Inoperative.

B. References

D. Lower Cargo Compartment DISCH or EXT Light Deactivation

SUBTASK 26-00-00-010-007 (1) Do this task: Cargo Fire Control Panel Removal, TASK 26-00-02-000-801. SUBTASK 26-00-00-020-005 (2) Do the steps that follow for an inoperative DISCH light circuit to make sure that the fire extinguisher bottles have an adequate charge: NOTE: The steps that follow are only applicable to the Boeing System. For systems installed by STC, see the STC holder. You will need a 28VDC circuit test lamp, with pigtail leads that terminate in pin contacts in an applicable holder. To make the lamp, lamp P/N 116-565-1042-001 (28 volt, 40 milliamps) or the equivalent is suggested, but you can use an applicable lamp or a digital multimeter. (a) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C01526 CARGO FIRE EXT 1 (b) Connect the test lamp or digital multimeter between the airplane side of connector D12760, pin 48 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 1) and pin 28 (pressure switch ground). (c) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C01526 CARGO FIRE EXT 1 (d) If the test lamp stays OFF or the digital multimeter shows an OPEN circuit, the fire extinguisher bottle has an adequate charge. (e) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C01526 CARGO FIRE EXT 1 (f) Remove the test lamp. (g) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C01526 CARGO FIRE EXT 1 26-00-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (h) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 (i) Connect the test lamp or digital multimeter between the airplane side of connector D12760, pin 45 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 2) and pin 28 (pressure switch ground). (j) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 (k) If the test lamp stays OFF or the digital multimeter shows an OPEN circuit, the fire extinguisher bottle has an adequate charge. (l) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 (m) Remove the test lamp. (n) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 16 C01528 CARGO FIRE EXT 2 RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-00-00-750-008WARNING KEEP PERSONNEL AWAY FROM THE EXTINGUISHER BOTTLE AREA DURING THE SQUIB CIRCUIT TEST . USE A MAXIMUM CURRENT OF 40 MILLIAMPS DURING THE TEST . USE ONLY TEST LAMP P/N 116-565-1042- 001 (28 VOLT , 40 MILLIAMPS) OR ITS EQUIVALENT . DO NOT USE A MULTIMETER. IF THE EXTINGUISHER BOTTLE RELEASES THE EXTINGUISHING MATERIAL, IT CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL. (3) Do the steps that follow to make sure that the EXT light SQUIB circuit operates correctly: NOTE: If the test lamp comes ON, the squib circuit is good. (a) Connect the test lamp between D12760 pin 48 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 1) and pin 26 (EXT 1 FWD squib). 1) Make sure that the test lamp comes on. (b) Connect the test lamp between D12760 pin 48 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 1) and pin 24 (EXT 1 AFT squib). 1) Make sure that the test lamp comes on. RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (c) Connect the test lamp between D12760 pin 45 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 2) and pin 12 (EXT 2 FWD squib). 26-00-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued) 1) Make sure that the test lamp comes on. (d) Connect the test lamp between D12760 pin 45 (the +28 volt source for Bottle No. 2) and pin 23 (EXT 2 AFT squib). 1) Make sure that the test lamp comes on. RYR ALL (e) Remove the test lamp. SUBTASK 26-00-00-410-004 (4) Do this task: Cargo Fire Control Panel Installation, TASK 26-00-02-400-801.

ATA 26 · 26-10-00

3 tasks
26-10-00-710-801Fire and Overheat Detection System - Operational Test ATA 26 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. Fire and Overheat Detection System - Operational Test (Figure 501 and Figure 502)

A. General

(1) The operational test makes sure that the Engine, Auxiliary Power Unit (APU), and Compartment Fire/Overheat Detection Systems operate correctly. The operational test uses only equipment that is installed on the airplane and it does not disturb the airplane systems. The operational test consists of these tests: (a) The FIRE/OVHT test for loops A and B. (b) The FIRE/OVHT test for loop A. (c) The FIRE/OVHT test for loop B. (d) The FAULT/INOP test for loops A and B. (e) The test for an Engine 1 Detection Fault. (f) The test for an Engine 2 Detection Fault. (g) The test for an APU Detection Fault. (h) The Air Mode Test for the P28 APU Remote Control Panel. (2) This test will do a check of all the systems affected by the detection test switches on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel. In other words, when you do the detection tests, you will look for every indication that is affected by the test. (3) A brief system description is included at the end of the procedure, refer to Table 501, Table 502, Table 503. (4) The Engine and APU Fire Detection Systems are monitored and controlled by the fire detection control module, M279, in the electrical and electronics compartment. The Compartment Overheat System is monitored and controlled by the compartment overheat detection controller, M237, in the electrical and electronics compartment. The module and the controller send fire, overheat, and fault signals to the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, and the P7 pilot's glareshield, in the flight compartment. The module also sends an APU fire signal to the P28 APU remote control panel, in the right main wheel well. (a) The Engine and APU Fire, Overheat, and Fault Warning Indications are as follows: 26-10-00 Table 501/26-10-00-993-805 System Condition Indication Location ENGINE 1 FIRE Engine 1 Fire Handle Light (red) P8-1 MASTER CAUTION Lights (amber) P7 FIRE WARN Lights (red) P7 Flight Compartment Fire Bell M315 ENGINE 2 FIRE Engine 2 Fire Handle Light (red) P8-1 MASTER CAUTION Lights (amber) P7 FIRE WARN Lights (red) P7 Flight Compartment Fire Bell M315 ENGINE 1 OVER-HEAT ENG 1 OVERHEAT Light (amber) P8-1 MASTER CAUTION Lights (amber) P7 OVHT/DET Light (amber) P7 ENGINE 2 OVER-HEAT ENG 2 OVERHEAT Light (amber) P8-1 MASTER CAUTION Lights (amber) P7 OVHT/DET Light (amber) P7 ENGINE 1 FAULT FAULT Light (amber) P8-1 ENGINE NO. 1 LOOP A Light (amber) M279 ENGINE NO. 1 LOOP B Light (amber) M279 ENGINE 2 FAULT FAULT Light (amber) P8-1 ENGINE NO. 2 LOOP A Light (amber) M279 ENGINE NO. 2 LOOP B Light (amber) M279 APU FIRE APU Fire Handle Light (red) P8-1 MASTER CAUTION Lights (amber) P7 FIRE WARN Lights (red) P7 Flight Compartment Fire Bell M315 Right Wheel Well APU Fire Warning Horn M277 APU Remote Fire Light (red) P28 26-10-00 Table 501/26-10-00-993-805 (Continued) System Condition Indication Location APU OVER-HEAT MASTER CAUTION Light (amber) P7 OVHT/DET Light (amber) P7 APU FAULT APU DET INOP Light (amber) P8-1 APU Light (amber) M279 (b) The Engine Fire Detection System has dual sensors in each loop and operates in "AND" logic. Therefore, both loops must sense a fire or a fault before the fire or fault indication is shown. The following table is a summary of the indications that will be given for the various conditions of the sensing loops: Table 502/26-10-00-993-806 LOOP 1 LOOP 2 INDICATIONS NORMAL NORMAL NONE NORMAL INOP NONE NORMAL FAULT NONE NORMAL FIRE NONE INOP NORMAL NONE INOP INOP NONE INOP FAULT FAULT INOP FIRE FIRE FAULT NORMAL NONE FAULT INOP FAULT FAULT FAULT FAULT FAULT FIRE FIRE FIRE NORMAL NONE FIRE INOP FIRE FIRE FAULT FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE (c) The following table shows all the fire, overheat, and fault indications in the flight compartment. These are the indications that will occur when the OVHT DET switches are in the normal position and you set the TEST switch to the FIRE/OVHT position, then to the NORMAL position, then to the FAULT INOP position, and then to the NORMAL position: Table 503/26-10-00-993-807 INDICATOR LOC FIRE/ OVHT NORMAL FAULT/ INOP NORMAL Engine "1" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF 26-10-00 Table 503/26-10-00-993-807 (Continued) INDICATOR LOC FIRE/ OVHT NORMAL FAULT/ INOP NORMAL Engine "2" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF "APU" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF "ENG 1 OVHT" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF "ENG 2 OVHT" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF "WHEEL WELL" Light P8-1 ON OFF OFF OFF "APU DET INOP" Light P8-1 OFF OFF ON OFF "FAULT" Light P8-1 OFF OFF ON OFF "FIRE WARN" Light *[1] P7 ON OFF OFF OFF "MASTER CAUTION" Light P7 ON *[2] ON *[2] "OVHT/DET" Light P7 ON *[2] ON *[2] Flight Compt Bell *[1] M315 ON OFF OFF OFF Wheel Well Bell *[1] M277 ON OFF OFF OFF Remote Fire Light *[1] P28 ON OFF OFF OFF *[1] Reset by pulling the "BELL CUTOUT" switch or by pushing the "FIRE/WARN" light, or by pushing the "HORN CUTOUT" switch on the P28 panel. *[2] Reset by pushing the "MASTER/CAUTION" light.

26-10-00-700-801Overheat Detection Test Using Handheld LCRZ Meter ATA 26 · Task 700-801

Task Description

3. Overheat Detection Test Using Handheld LCRZ Meter (Figure 503)

A. General

(1) This task consists of the following procedures using a 1v/1kHz AC LCRZ impedance meter: (a) Instructions for the health check of the compartment overheat loop detectors and wiring. 1) This procedure is used to detect shorts, open, and degraded overheat loop detectors in the wing and body overheat detection system. (b) Instructions for locating faulty detector or wiring for compartment overheat loop that reads less than nominal range. 1) This procedure is used to isolate an entire loop to a degraded or failed detector, or to a wiring problem.

B. References

Reference Title 22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401) 22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 26-18-01-000-801 Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit Removal (P/B 401) 26-18-01-400-801 Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit Installation (P/B 401) 26-18-02-000-801 Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Sensor Element - Removal (P/B 401) 26-18-02-400-801 Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Sensor Element - Installation (P/B 401) WDM 26-12-11 Wiring Diagram Manual

F. Overheat Detection Loop Health Check with COM-17158 SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-004 (1) Connect the black lead to aircraft ground. 26-10-00 SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-005 (2) Connect the red test lead to the breakout jack on the front panel of the overheat detection module extender box assembly, C26007-3, -73, SPL-12922, as identified in Table 504 (View C, Figure 503). Table 504/26-10-00-993-808 Overheat Detection Loop Health Check Compartment Overheat Detection Controller Receptacle D742 Pin *[1] Expected Reading (Impedance) 2 22k ohms +/- 5k 4 21k ohms +/- 5k 8 22k ohms +/- 5k 9 25k ohms +/- 5k 10 14k ohms +/- 5k 11 14k ohms +/- 5k 12 21k ohms +/- 5k 13 14k ohms +/- 5k 21 25k ohms +/- 5k 24 25k ohms +/- 5k 27 14k ohms +/- 5k 29 25k ohms +/- 5k *[1] Refer to WDM 26-12-11. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-001 (3) Prepare the loop controller, COM-17158. (a) For each measurement do these steps: 1) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading. 2) Set the switch to TEST then back to ISOL to reset unit. 3) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading. 4) Make sure that both readings are similar to provide accurate results. (b) Check for a short or degraded detector by taking the measurements per Table 504. 1) If a reading is greater than the expected reading, check the wiring between the D742 connector and the first detector in the loop for an open circuit condition. NOTE: A greater reading is permitted if the overheat detection unit BITE test passes. 2) If a reading is between 5k ohms and the nominal range, a scheduled repeat test for degradation or replacement of the detector is recommended at the operator's convenience. NOTE: This indicates that one of the detectors is degraded. The degraded detector may continue to degrade resulting in intermittent failures until it completely fails, which is typically within 180 days. a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”. 26-10-00 3) If a reading is < 5k ohms, do the following to isolate the problem: NOTE: This indicates that either one of the detectors is shorted between the center conductor and ground, or that there is a problem in the wiring. a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”. SUBTASK 26-10-00-080-001 (4) Disconnect the black test lead from ground. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-002 (7) Set the loop controller, COM-17158, switch to CONT . (a) For each measurement, do these steps: 1) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading. 2) Set the switch to TEST then back to CONT to reset unit. 3) Allow reading to stabilize and record reading. 4) Make sure that both readings are similar to provide accurate results. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-003 (8) Check for an open detector or circuit by taking the measurements per Table 505. (a) If the reading is greater than the values shown in Table 505, do continuity checks of the applicable loop segments to isolate the failed loop or wiring. (b) Replace the faulty detector or repair wiring, as applicable.

NOTE: A greater reading is permitted if the overheat detection unit BITE test passes. 2) If a reading is between 5k ohms and the nominal range, a scheduled repeat test for degradation or replacement of the detector is recommended at the operator's convenience. NOTE: This indicates that one of the detectors is degraded. The degraded detector may continue to degrade resulting in intermittent failures until it completely fails, which is typically within 180 days. a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”. 3) If a reading is < 5k ohms, do the following to isolate the problem: NOTE: This indicates that either one of the detectors is shorted between the center conductor and ground, or that there is a problem in the wiring. a) To isolate to the degraded detector, proceed to section “Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop”. 4) If all of the readings are within tolerance, continue to the next breakout jack per Table 504. (a) If the reading is greater than the values shown in Table 505, do continuity checks of the applicable loop segments to isolate the failed loop or wiring. NOTE: If the reading is greater than the values shown in Table 505, it is possible that it can be because of dirty connections or corrosion on the leads. (b) Replace the faulty detector or repair wiring, as applicable.

I. Locating Faulty Detector or Wiring For Overheat Detection Loop

SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-011 (1) For the applicable loop section, disconnect the loop at the approximate half way point (WDM 26-12-11). NOTE: This will allow you to determine which side is faulty. For example, for the loop that connects to pins 4 and 12 of connector D742, disconnect between detectors M1761 and M268. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-012 (2) Check the readings again at each pin at the rack connector. (a) The side that shows the lower reading is the faulty side. (b) The resistance values should roughly double from the original expected value. NOTE: For example, if the original expected reading was 21k ohms ± 5k ohms and you measured 5k ohms, when you disconnect the loops at the halfway point, the resistance should be +42k ohms on each side on a good system. If one side reads 42k ohms, and the other side reads 5k ohms, the faulty side is the one reading 5k ohms. 1) Note the faulty side, and reconnect the loop segments that were disconnected in the previous step. 2) Disconnect the loop at the approximate half way point of the faulty side. 3) Check the readings again at each pin at the connector rack. a) The side that has the lower reading is the faulty side. 4) If it is necessary, continue to check to isolate to the faulty loop section. 5) Replace the faulty detector (TASK 26-18-02-000-801, TASK 26-18-02-400-801), or repair wiring (Standard Wiring Practices Manual (SWPM)), as applicable. NOTE: Value for individual loops is 100k ohms, but this number varies by length. A very long section, 200+ inches, could read 100k ohms or below. 26-10-00

SUBTASK 26-10-00-420-001 (2) If flight control computer B was removed, install the flight control computer B (TASK 22-11-33-400-801). SUBTASK 26-10-00-410-001 (3) Install the compartment overheat detection control unit (TASK 26-18-01-400-801).

E. Prepare for the Test

SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-014 (1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. NOTE: Electrical power must be off to prevent incorrect fault indications or fault messages from occurring. SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-015 (2) Make sure that the battery switch is in the OFF position on the P-5 overhead panel. SUBTASK 26-10-00-860-016 (3) Make sure that all ground power is disconnected. SUBTASK 26-10-00-020-003 (4) Remove the M237 compartment overheat detection control unit (TASK 26-18-01-000-801). SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-013 (5) Insert the interface cable, SPL-17990, to the M237 compartment overheat detection control unit's connector on the airplane, with the optional electronic dust cap to verify if there is any power on the connector. (a) If the electronic dust cap has power, remove the power. 26-10-00 SUBTASK 26-10-00-020-004 (6) If no power is confirmed, remove the electronic dust cap and connect the wiring tester, COM-17989, to the interface cable, SPL-17990. (a) Do not ground the test box unit for the bleed overheat loop test. NOTE: This may introduce error on the overheat loop readings. SUBTASK 26-10-00-480-014 (7) Log into the tablet to provide testing and traceability. (a) Start up the tablet and login with your user ID. (b) Identify the location of the aircraft being maintained. (c) Select the aircraft model. (d) Select the aircraft subsystem (example: bleed OH loop). (e) Identify the reason for your test (training/diagnostic/preventative). (f) The software will now guide you into the set-up with pictograph. F. Overheat Detection Test with UN-ATE NOTE: Do not ground the test box unit for the bleed overheat loop test. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-013 (1) UN-ATE will make a scan to provide an overview of all OH loops. (a) Select “Scan” on the wiring tester, COM-17989, software on the tablet. 1) The wiring tester, COM-17989, will automatically make impedance measurements on each loop circuit and present results as per wiring diagram. NOTE: Each loop circuit will have three impedance measurements such as the Table 506 below. Table 506/26-10-00-993-810 Overheat Loop Impedance Measurements Measurement type Impedance result Inner to inner core Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) One side inner to ground Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) Other side inner to ground Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) 2) The wiring tester, COM-17989, will analyze the three measurement results of each loop circuit and highlight in color the problematic results based on the following condition scenarios in Table 507. Table 507/26-10-00-993-811 Overheat Detection Loop Health Check Wiring Example Condition Scenarios Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) Loop health status Reference procedure step Loop Inner to Inner core 1 < 12 ohms *[1] - Good inner core - 2 > 12 ohms *[1] - Outside tolerance Step (3) 3 Any reading 1 to 100 nF Opened inner core Step (4) Loop Inner to ground on both sides 4 1 M ohms- 1 to 100 nF Excellent loop Step (2) 5 < 15 k ohms 1 to 100 nF Shortened bad loop Step (5) 26-10-00 *[1] The resistance values provided here are only arbitrary. The prescribed maximum tolerance values for each circuit will be populated into the baseline signature reference. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-016 (2) Healthy loop - All measurements are within their tolerance. (a) Once the scan completed, wiring tester, COM-17989, shows NO HIGHLIGHTon any loop circuit indicating all loop circuit are HEALTHY . (b) Go to Step (7). SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-015 (3) Loop inner core measurement outside of tolerance. (a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags an OUT OF TOLERANCE inner to inner core loop circuit. 1) This problem may be an indication of the degrading wire/loop connection (corrosion, pin insert, etc). 2) The procedure is to validate with conventional procedures and visual inspection. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-014 (4) Opened inner core loop fault location procedure. (a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags an OPENED or discontinuity along the loop inner core. NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, fault detection feature is used to locate the discontinuity point. 1) Select the problematic loop and select FIRELOOP DIAGNOSTIC and then IDENTITY BAD LOOP to locate the closest point to the open fault location. NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, diagnostic feature may take up to ten minutes to process. Once the process is completed, the closest overheat fireloop segment causing the fault is highlighted. 2) Confirm the damage part (wire or fireloop segment) that cause the problem, do these steps: a) Go to the fireloop segment located by the wiring tester, COM-17989, and do a check for any visible damage that may cause the discontinuity in the loop. b) Manually disconnect one side of the fireloop segment closest to the wiring tester, COM-17989, located point. c) Connect the INNER core lead at both sides of the connector to the ground (aircraft chassis) using two short cables with alligator clips and lead adaptors. NOTE: This location is the manually grounded point. d) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software select REFRESH IMPEDANCE to re-measure the three impedance measurements of the selected loop (inner to inner core and inner to ground x 2). e) The next step confirms if the fault is located at the RIGHTor LEFT side of the previously grounded point (Table 508). NOTE: Table locates the fault segment according to the measurement results. 26-10-00 Table 508/26-10-00-993-812 Opened Inner Core Loop Check Scenario Measurement Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) Fault Location - Inner to inner - - - 1 1st Inner to ground XX ohm - Fault at the RIGHT side of the grounded point2nd Inner to ground - XX nF 2 1st Inner to ground - XX nF Fault at the LEFT side of the grounded point2nd Inner to ground XX ohm - f) Go to the previously grounded point undo the grounding and reconnect the disconnected fireloop segment. g) Move to the next wire/fireloop segment (RIGHTor LEFT according to the previous deduction) and disconnect one side of the that segment and connect the inner core leads to the ground. NOTE: This location is the second grounding point. h) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software redo the measurement by selecting REFRESH IMPEDANCE. i) Confirm the side (RIGHTor LEFT) of the faulty segment. NOTE: After this step, the faulty part may be precisely located. In some cases, other disconnecting and grounding processes may be required, especially in cases where multiple wire segments are inserted between overheat fireloop segments. j) Once the faulty part located, confirmed its condition using conventional Boeing or operator procedure. k) Replace or repair the part. l) Do the complete scan in Step (1) again to confirm the repair. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-017 (5) Shorted bad loop fault location procedure. (a) The wiring tester, COM-17989, flags a SHORTED loop or alteration of the loop insulation quality. NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, fault detection feature is used to locate the short point. (b) Select the problematic loop and select FIRELOOP DIAGNOSTIC and then IDENTIFY BAD LOOP to locate the closest point to the short fault location. NOTE: The wiring tester, COM-17989, diagnostic feature may take up to ten minutes to process. Once the process is completed, the closest wire/fireloop segment causing the fault is highlighted. (c) Confirm the damage part (wire or fireloop segment) that cause the problem, do these steps: 1) Go to the wire/fireloop segment located by the wiring tester, COM-17989, and check for any visible damage that may cause the short in the loop. 2) Manually disconnect one side of the fireloop segment closest to the wiring tester, COM-17989, located point. NOTE: This location is the floating point. 26-10-00 3) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software select REFRESH IMPEDANCE to measure the three impedance measurements of the selected loop (inner to inner core and inner to ground x 2). 4) The next step confirms if the fault is located at the RIGHTor LEFT side of the previously floating point (Table 509). NOTE: Table locates the fault segment according to the measurement results. Table 509/26-10-00-993-813 Shorted Bad Loop Check Scenario Measurement Resistance (ohm) Capacitance (nF) Fault Location - Inner to inner - - - 1 1st Inner to ground Short Resistance ohm - Fault at the LEFT side of the floating point2nd Inner to ground - XX nF 2 1st Inner to ground - XX nF Fault at the LEFT side of the floating point2nd Inner to ground Short Resistance ohm 5) Go to the previously floating point and reconnect the disconnected wire/fireloop segment. 6) Move to the next wire/fireloop segment (RIGHTor LEFT according to the previous deduction) and disconnect one side of that segment. NOTE: This location is the second floating point. 7) In the wiring tester, COM-17989, software redo the measurement by selecting REFRESH IMPEDANCE. 8) Confirm the side (RIGHTor LEFT) of the faulty segment. NOTE: After this step, the faulty part may be precisely located. In some cases, other disconnecting processes may be required, especially if the short resistance is between 10 k ohms to 15 k ohms. 9) Once the faulty part located, confirmed its condition using conventional Boeing/Airline procedures. 10) Replace or repair part. 11) Do the complete scan in Step (1) again to confirm the repair. SUBTASK 26-10-00-700-019 (6) Analysis and documentation. (a) You have now completed the wiring tester, COM-17989, diagnostic and should document your work. 1) Insert your detailed comments about the repairs performed in the wiring tester, COM-17989, test notes (optional). 2) Save the test results. a) Click on the proper tab that best describes the diagnostic tests results. NOTE: These comments are preset and will be correlated to the reason of your test in the Prepare for the Test Section, Step (6) (not relevant, no issue found, found my problem, potential degradation). b) The wiring tester, COM-17989, will save it using traceability data (aircraft type, sub-system, serial number, date). 26-10-00

G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 26-10-00-080-007 (1) Disconnect the wiring tester, COM-17989, from the interface cable, SPL-17990. SUBTASK 26-10-00-080-008 (2) Disconnect the interface cable, SPL-17990, from the M237 compartment overheat detection control unit connector on the airplane. SUBTASK 26-10-00-420-002 (3) Install the M237 compartment overheat detection control unit (TASK 26-18-01-400-801). SUBTASK 26-10-00-840-002 (4) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.

26-11-00-710-801Engine Fire Detection - Operational Test ATA 26 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. Engine Fire Detection - Operational Test (Figure 501)

A. General

(1) The operational test does a check of the engine fire detection system without disturbing the detection system and it only uses the equipment that is on the airplane. This test will verify the circuit from the P8-1 engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire control panel to the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279. The test will make sure that the engine and APU fire detection control module, that continuously monitors the detection loops, operates correctly.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

(b) On the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The ENGINE 1 (2) LOOP A FAULT AREA light comes on. 2) The ENGINE 1 (2) LOOP B FAULT AREA light comes on. 3) The three FAULT CODE lights are OFF-ON-OFF . SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-008 (3) On the test box, move the ENGINE LOOP A/1 AND B/2 GND FAULT switches to the NORMAL position. (a) Make sure that the FAULT light, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, goes off. (b) On the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The ENGINE 1 (2) LOOP A FAULT AREA light goes off. 2) The ENGINE 1 (2) LOOP B FAULT AREA light goes off. 3) The three FAULT CODE lights are OFF-OFF-OFF .

H. Overheat Indication Circuit Test

SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-009 (1) On the test box, move the ENGINE LOOP A/1 AND B/2 OVHT switches to the OVHT position. (a) Make sure that the ENG 1 (2) OVERHEAT light, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, comes on. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The captain and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights comes on. 2) The OVHT DET light comes on. SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-010 (2) On the test box, move the ENGINE LOOP A/1 AND B/2 OVHT switches to the NORMAL position. (a) Make sure that the ENG 1 (2) OVERHEAT light, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, goes off. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The captain and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights goes off. 2) The OVHT DET light goes off.

I. Fire Indication Circuit Test

SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-011 (1) On the test box, move the ENGINE LOOP A/1 AND B/2 FIRE switches to the FIRE position. (a) Make sure that the engine 1 (2) Fire Handle light, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, comes on. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these conditions occur: 26-11-00 1) The captain and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights comes on. 2) The captain and first officer's FIRE WARN lights comes on. (c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, on the aural warning module, M315, comes on. RYR 372-999 (d) Make sure that the lights on the left and right engine start levers, on the P10 engine start lever panel, comes on. RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-012 (2) On the test box, move the ENGINE LOOP A/1 AND B/2 FIRE switches to the NORMAL position. (a) Make sure that the engine 1 (2) Fire Handle light, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, goes off. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The captain and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights goes off. 2) The captain and first officer's FIRE WARN lights goes off. (c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, on the aural warning module, M315, stops. RYR 372-999 (d) Make sure that the lights on the left and right engine start levers, on the P10 engine start lever panel, turns off. RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-11-00-480-002 (3) On Engine 1 (2), disconnect the test box equipment, SPL-1655, from the two terminal lugs (A/1 and B/2) of the FAN LOWER Fire Detector, M1758, and airplane ground. SUBTASK 26-11-00-730-013 (4) Make sure that you do the circuit test for engine 1 and engine 2.

Task Description

2. Engine Fire Detector Inspection (Figure 601)

A. References

Reference Title SWPM 20-10-11 WIRING ASSEMBLY AND INSTALLATION CONFIGURATION

C. Engine Fire Detector Inspection

SUBTASK 26-11-01-210-001 (1) Do a visual check of the fire detectors for nicks, cracks, cuts, burrs, corrosion, breaks, scoring, deformation, dents, thread damage, or other defects. (a) Make sure that the markings and nomenclature are complete and easy to read. SUBTASK 26-11-01-211-001 (2) Make sure that the ceramic insulation around the terminal stud of the fire detectors is not damaged. NOTE: Refer to the manufacturer’s Component Maintenance Manual (CMM) for permitted defects. SUBTASK 26-11-01-211-002 (3) Do a visual check of the sensor element for abrasion, kinks, cuts, or severe pits. NOTE: Chafing damage shows a clearance problem in the airplane installation. NOTE: If the sensor element tube has no gas leak, small dents, nicks, or pits are permitted. NOTE: The fire detector will fail the internal resistance test if there is a gas leak SUBTASK 26-11-01-212-001 (4) Make sure that there is no contact between the forward transponder of the upper fan case engine fire/overheat detector and strut insulation blanket (View F , Figure 601). SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-001 (5) If there is a contact and wire insulation is damaged, repair the wire as described below and change the routing of the wire (View F , Figure 601). (a) If no contact is found and the wires are routed, no further action is required. SUBTASK 26-11-01-030-001 (6) Disconnect the lug from the fire/overheat transponder. 26-11-01 SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-002 (7) Cut a sufficient length of TFE-R 5/8-X tubing, G50541, or TFE-R-1/2-X tubing, G50544, to cover the worn area. (a) Make sure that the sleeve long enough to exceed the length of the damage by approximately 0.4 in. (10.2 mm). SUBTASK 26-11-01-300-003 (8) Heat the sleeve at 700°F (371°C) maximum until shrinking operation is completed. SUBTASK 26-11-01-430-001 (9) Reconnect the lug on the fire detection transponder. (a) For the fire detector harness, MW0316, make sure that the minimum clearance between the fire detection wires and strut insulation blanket is 0.15 in. (3.81 mm). 1) If the clearance between the fire detection wires and strut insulation is not within the limits, do the following steps: a) Adjust the wires. b) Secure per SWPM 20-10-11.

26-11-02-000-801Fan Fire Detection Harness Removal ATA 26 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Fan Fire Detection Harness Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) The removal task for harness MW0315 and MW0316 is the same. (2) MW0315 is a component of loop A of the engine fan fire detection circuit. (3) MW0316 is a component of loop B of the engine fan fire detection circuit.

B. References

Reference Title 78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 411 Engine 1 - Engine 421 Engine 2 - Engine

D. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 26-11-02-040-001 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2 A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1 SUBTASK 26-11-02-010-001 WARNING DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (2) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00. SUBTASK 26-11-02-010-002 (3) Get access to the harness in the 3:00 o'clock strut as follows: 26-11-02 (a) Remove the screws [110] which attach the 3:00 O'clock strut fairing [111] to the 3:00 o'clock strut. (b) Remove the 3:00 O'clock strut fairing [111]. (c) Remove the bolts [104] which attach the upper half-firewall [103] to the lower half-firewall [109]. (d) Remove the upper half-firewall [103]. (e) Remove the metal plug [125] from the upper half-firewall [103]. (f) Remove the bolts [108] which attach the lower half-firewall [109] to the strut. (g) Remove the lower half-firewall [109]. (h) Remove the bolts [106] and washers [107] which attach the 3:00 O'clock strut seal [105]. (i) Remove the 3:00 O'clock strut seal [105].

E. Fan Fire Detection Harness Removal

SUBTASK 26-11-02-020-001 (1) Fan fire detection harness removal: (a) Disconnect the harness connector (core) [118] from the core fire detection harness receptacle. (b) Disconnect the harness connector (pylon) [119] from the pylon receptacle. (c) Remove the nut [114] and washer [115] which attach the two terminal lugs of each harness to the fan fire detectors. (d) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0315 [101], remove the bolt [112] and washer [113] that attach each ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket. (e) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], do these steps: 1) Remove the nut [121] and washer [122] from the one end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102] (View F , Figure 401). 2) Remove the bolt [123] and washer [124] from the another end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102] (View H, Figure 401). (f) Loosen the bolt [120] and open the clamp [116] that attach each harness to the engine brackets. (g) Disengage the harness from the omega clamps. (h) Pull the flexible clamp with harness out of the 3:00 o'clock strut and disengage the harness. (i) Pull the harness through the 3:00 o'clock strut. (j) Remove the harness from the engine.

G. Fan Fire Detection Harness Installation

SUBTASK 26-11-02-420-001 (1) Install the fan fire detection harness: (a) Put the harness in position on the fan frame. (b) Install the harness through the 3:00 o'clock strut to the inside of the engine. (c) Distribute the slack all along the attachment points on the fan frame and in the 3:00 o'clock strut. (d) Connect the harness connector to the core fire detection harness receptacle. 1) Tighten by hand. (e) Connect the harness connector to the pylon receptacle. 1) Tighten by hand. (f) For the fire detector harness, MW0315, do these steps: 1) Separate the wires as much as possible. 2) Bend the wires down and away from contact with the strut blanket. (g) For the fire detector harness, MW0316, do these steps: 1) Achieve the minimum clearance of 0.15 in. (3.81 mm) between the fire detection wires and strut insulation blanket, do the following steps: a) Adjust the wires. b) Secure the wires (SWPM 20-10-11). 2) Make sure that the minimum clearance between the fire detection wires and strut insulation blanket is 0.15 in. (3.81 mm). CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT YOU HAVE THE HARNESS IN THE CORRECT POSITION IN THE FIREWALL OPEN HOLES. IF YOU DO NOT INSTALL HARNESS CORRECTLY , DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (h) Attach the terminal lugs of the harness to each fan fire detector with the washer [115] that should be installed under the nut [114]. NOTE: If the leads are different sizes, install the lead with the small hole on top of the lead with the large hole. 1) Tighten the nut [114] to 25.00 in-lb (2.82 N·m) - 35.00 in-lb (3.95 N·m). (i) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0315 [101], attach the ground lug of the harness to each engine bracket with the washer [113] and bolt [112]. 1) Tighten the bolts [112] to 97 in-lb (11 N·m) - 103 in-lb (11.6 N·m). (j) For the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], do these steps: 1) For the one end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], attach the ground lug of the harness to engine bracket with the washer [122] and nut [121] as follows (View F , Figure 401): 26-11-02 a) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the contact surface of the engine bracket and ground lug. b) Attach the ground lug of the harness to engine bracket with the washer [122] and nut [121]. c) Tighten the nut [121] to 90 in-lb (10 N·m) - 105 in-lb (11.9 N·m). d) Measure the electrical resistance between the ground lug and airplane structure (SWPM 20-20-00). <1> Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614. <2> Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less. 2) For the another end of the fan fire detection harness, MW0316 [102], attach the ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket with the bolt [123] and washer [124] as follows (View H, Figure 401): a) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the contact surface of the engine bracket, ground lug, and washer [124]. b) Attach the ground lug of the harness to the engine bracket with the bolt [123] and washer [124]. c) Tighten the bolt [123] to 78 in-lb (9 N·m) - 82 in-lb (9.3 N·m). d) Measure the electrical resistance between the ground lug and airplane structure (SWPM 20-20-00). <1> Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614. <2> Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less. (k) Attach the harness to each engine bracket with the clamp [116] and tighten the bolt [120]. (l) Install the harness in the flexible clamp at the 3:00 o'clock strut location. (m) Engage the harness in the omega clamps. SUBTASK 26-11-02-410-001 (2) Close the access to the 3:00 o'clock position strut as follows: (a) Install the 3:00 O'clock strut seal [105], washers [107], and bolts [106]. 1) Tighten the bolts [106] to 110.0 in-lb (12.4 N·m) - 120.0 in-lb (13.6 N·m). CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE HARNESSES ARE IN THE CORRECT POSITION IN THE FIREWALLS. IF THE HARNESSES ARE NOT IN THE CORRECT POSITION, IT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT . (b) Install the lower half-firewall [109] and attach it to the strut with the bolts [108]. 1) Use graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101], to lubricate the threads of the bolts [108]. 2) Tighten the bolts [108] to 109.25 in-lb (12.34 N·m) - 120.75 in-lb (13.64 N·m). (c) Install the metal plug [125]. (d) Install the upper half-firewall [103] and attach it to the lower half fire wall with the bolts [104]. 1) Use graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101], to lubricate the threads of the bolts [104]. 26-11-02 2) Tighten the bolts [104] to 109.25 in-lb (12.34 N·m) - 120.75 in-lb (13.64 N·m). SUBTASK 26-14-00-860-003 (1) Make sure that the green power indicator light on the lavatory smoke detector is on, if applicable. NOTE: Some detectors have plugged green power indicator with plug-button. RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-14-00-730-007 (2) Use a smoke generator, COM-11202, to make smoke adjacent to the lavatory smoke detector. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS SUBTASK 26-14-00-750-004 (3) Make sure that the red alarm light on the lavatory smoke detector comes on. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS PU90-461 OR PU90-499 SUBTASK 26-14-00-750-006 (4) Make sure that you hear the smoke detector alarm horn in the lavatory. NOTE: The smoke detector PU90–461 has pulsating alarm horn sound. The smoke detector PU90–499 has steady alarm horn sound. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS PU90-429 SUBTASK 26-14-00-750-008 (5) Make sure that these forward cabin attendants' panel alarm indications occur (Figure 502): 26-14-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS PU90-429 (Continued) (a) SMOKE DETECTOR panel: 1) You can hear the alarm horn. 2) The SMOKE DETECT light flashes. 3) The applicable location light flashes. SUBTASK 26-14-00-730-006 (6) Push the HORN INTERRUPT and RESET switch on the control panel. (a) Make sure that all of the external alarm indications stop. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS PU90-461 OR PU90-499 SUBTASK 26-14-00-730-003 (7) Push the ALARM INTERRUPT switch on the smoke detector with an applicable tool. (a) Make sure that all of the alarm indications stop. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS SUBTASK 26-14-00-860-004 (8) Clear the smoke from the lavatory. NOTE: If the smoke is not cleared from the lavatory within 60 seconds, the smoke indications will occur again. RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-14-00-730-005 (9) Do the Smoke Test again for each of the other lavatory smoke detectors. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS

26-14-01-000-801Lavatory Smoke Detector Removal ATA 26 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Lavatory Smoke Detector Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) This task gives instructions to remove the lavatory smoke detector.

B. References

Reference Title 26-14-01-100-801 Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning (P/B 701)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 200 Upper Half of Fuselage

D. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 26-14-01-860-003 (1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name E 12 C01125 LAVATORY SMOKE

E. Lavatory Smoke Detector Removal

SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-002 (1) Loosen the face plate screws [3] on the face plate [4] of the smoke detector [11]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-003 (2) Remove the face plate [4] from the smoke detector case [7]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-010 (3) Remove the washers [18] from the face plate screws [3] that are attached to the face plate [4]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-004 (4) Remove the mounting screws [5] that hold the smoke detector [11] to the ceiling bracket [1]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-005 (5) Disconnect the electrical connector [6] on the smoke detector [11] from the airplane electrical connector. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-006 (6) Remove the smoke detector [11] and ceiling bracket [1] from the ceiling. NOTE: You do not have to remove the ceiling bracket from the smoke detector. The new smoke detector will usually have a ceiling bracket attached to it. 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS (Continued) SUBTASK 26-14-01-160-003 (7) If it is necessary, do this task: Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning, TASK 26-14-01-100-801.

(b) If the ceiling bracket [1] is not connected to the smoke detector [11], install the ceiling bracket [1] to the smoke detector [11] with the mounting screws [5]. 26-14-01-100-801Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning ATA 26 · Task 100-801

Task Description

2. Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning (Figure 701) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. References

Reference Title 26-14-00-730-801 Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (P/B 501)

B. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description STD-1086 Gloves - Rubber STD-3939 Air Source - Regulated, Dry Filtered, 0 to 10 psig

C. Consumable Materials

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 200 Upper Half of Fuselage 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

E. Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning

SUBTASK 26-14-01-860-001 (1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name E 12 C01125 LAVATORY SMOKE 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS (Continued) SUBTASK 26-14-01-160-001 WARNING WHEN YOU CLEAN THE SMOKE DETECTOR AND SENSOR, MAKE SURE THAT THE AREA HAS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. KEEP THE ALCOHOL AWAY FROM HEAT , SPARKS OR FLAMES. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE ALCOHOL. PUT ON RUBBER GLOVES. ALCOHOL IS A FLAMMABLE AND POISONOUS SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (2) Put on the rubber glove, STD-1086. SUBTASK 26-14-01-160-002 (3) Remove the dirt from the exterior of the case cover [1] with the cotton wiper, G00034, soaked with alcohol, B00065. SUBTASK 26-14-01-020-001 (4) Do these steps to remove and disassemble the smoke sensor assembly [4]: (a) Insert the pocket scale between the smoke sensor assembly [4] and case cover [1]. (b) Push the spring lever [2] with the pocket scale and pull out the smoke sensor assembly [4]. (c) Slide the screwdriver between each side of the sensor cell assembly [6] and cover [7] and twist the screwdriver to release the lockpins. (d) Remove the cover [7] from the sensor cell assembly [6]. WARNING DO NOT DISASSEMBLE THE SENSOR CELL ASSEMBLY . THE SENSOR CELL CONTAINS RADIOACTIVE MATERIAL. THE RADIOACTIVE MATERIAL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL. CAUTION DO NOT TOUCH THE ELECTRODE IN THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY . DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR CAN OCCUR. (e) Pull the mesh [8] from the sensor cell assembly [6]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-210-001 (5) Do these steps to clean the mesh [8] and sensor cell assembly [6]: (a) Examine the mesh [8] for contamination. (b) To remove contamination, clean the mesh [8] with general purpose household detergent cleaner, B00541, and warm water. (c) If the mesh [8] is very dirty, replace it with a new mesh [8]. (d) With the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, dry the mesh [8] completely. CAUTION BLOW THE AIR LIGHTLY INTO THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY . TOO MUCH FORCE CAN CAUSE STATIC ELECTRICITY ON THE ELECTRODES WHICH CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR. (e) With the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, lightly blow any dirt or particles out of the sensor cell assembly [6]. 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH JAMCO IONIZATION DETECTORS (Continued) SUBTASK 26-14-01-420-001 (6) Do these steps to assemble and install the smoke sensor assembly [4]: (a) Install the mesh [8] on the sensor cell assembly [6]. (b) Align the hole in the cover [7] with the hole in the sensor cell assembly [6]. (c) Install the cover [7] on the sensor cell assembly [6]. (d) Install the smoke sensor assembly [4] in the case cover [1]. SUBTASK 26-14-01-860-002 (7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name E 12 C01125 LAVATORY SMOKE SUBTASK 26-14-01-710-001 (8) Do this task: Lavatory Smoke Detection - Smoke Test, TASK 26-14-00-730-801.

3. Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning (Figure 702)

SUBTASK 26-14-01-000-003 (1) Do this task: Lavatory Smoke Detector Removal (Kidde), TASK 26-14-01-000-802. SUBTASK 26-14-01-000-004 CAUTION BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE OR INSTALL THE SCREEN OF THE COVER ASSEMBLY AND LABYRINTH. THIS WILL HELP PREVENT SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH. SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH CAN CAUSE STRAY LIGHT REFLECTIONS AND INCORRECT CALIBRATIONS TO OCCUR. CAUTION ESDS COMPONENTS AND UNITS MUST BE PROTECTED FROM STATIC DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES. (2) Disassemble the lavatory smoke detector as follows: (a) Remove the screws [13] and washers [14] that attach the cover/Electro-Magnetic Interference (EMI) gasket assembly to the housing/screen assembly [11]. 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued) (b) Remove the slide-lock-post [16] and washer [17] that attach the bonding jumper [19] to the connector [18]. NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one washer, one hex nut, and one slide-lock-post. (c) Remove the remaining slide-lock-post [16] and washer [17] that attach the connector [18] to the housing/screen assembly [11]. NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one hex nut, one lock washer, one flat washer, and one slide-lock-post. (d) Pull the connector [18] back inside the housing/screen assembly [11]. (e) Remove the gasket [20]. (f) Remove the EMI gasket [28] from the cover [15]. 1) If it is necessary, remove the nut [31], lock washer [30], and flat washer [29] to disconnect the bonding jumper [32] from the cover/EMI gasket assembly. (g) Remove the screws [21] and washers [22] that connect the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12] to the housing/screen assembly [11]. (h) Remove the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12]. (i) Disconnect the connector [27] from the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12]. (j) Remove the fasteners [23], washers [24], and nuts [25] that attach the labyrinth top [26] to the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12]. F. Lavatory Smoke Detector Cleaning SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-003 CAUTION ESDS COMPONENTS AND UNITS MUST BE PROTECTED FROM STATIC DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES. (1) Clean the labyrinth assembly. (a) Make a solution of soap and water with one part soap, B50026, and twenty parts water, G50527. WARNING WHEN YOU USE THE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN, TO COOL OR TO DRY , DO NOT EXCEED 30 PSIG (207 KPA). MAKE SURE YOU WEAR EYE PROTECTION AND DO NOT DIRECT COMPRESSED AIR AT YOURSELF AND OTHERS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. CAUTION DO NOT PUT THE LABYRINTH FULLY IN THE SOAP AND WATER SOLUTION WHEN YOU CLEAN THE LABYRINTH. LARGE QUANTITIES OF THE SOAP AND WATER SOLUTION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE LABYRINTH. (b) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, and dry, clean air source 0 to 60 psi, STD-4107, at maximum pressure of 30 psig (207 kPa) to remove the unwanted material from the inner area of the labyrinth. NOTE: You can also use the soap and water solution. Remove the soap and water solution with clean water. 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued) (c) Clean the inner area of the labyrinth with a clean cotton swab, STD-6402, moist with the soap and water solution. (d) Dry the lenses with a clean and dry cotton swab, STD-6402. (e) Remove the remaining soap or contamination from the lenses. (f) Make sure that the labyrinth assembly is fully dry before you assemble the smoke detector. SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-004 (2) If it is necessary, clean the screen of the housing/screen assembly [11]. WARNING WHEN YOU USE THE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN, TO COOL OR TO DRY , DO NOT EXCEED 30 PSIG (207 KPA). MAKE SURE YOU WEAR EYE PROTECTION AND DO NOT DIRECT COMPRESSED AIR AT YOURSELF AND OTHERS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. (a) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, or dry, clean air source 0 to 60 psi, STD-4107, at maximum pressure of 30 psig (207 kPa) to remove unwanted materials from the screen. SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-005 (3) Do these steps to clean all non-electrical parts: WARNING ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS FLAMMABLE. AVOID PROLONGED BREATHING OF VAPOR AND PROLONGED OR REPEATED CONTACT WITH SKIN. AVOID CONTACT WITH EYES. CLEAN PARTS IN WELL- VENTILATED AREA AND USE APPROVED SAFETY EQUIPMENT . (a) Clean the external surfaces with lint-free cloth, G01043, moist with alcohol, B50073. (b) Dry the surfaces with dry lint-free cloth, G01043. SUBTASK 26-14-01-100-006 CAUTION ESDS COMPONENTS AND UNITS MUST BE PROTECTED FROM STATIC DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES. (4) Clean the connectors and other electrical components as follows: WARNING ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS FLAMMABLE. AVOID PROLONGED BREATHING OF VAPOR AND PROLONGED OR REPEATED CONTACT WITH SKIN. AVOID CONTACT WITH EYES. CLEAN PARTS IN WELL- VENTILATED AREA AND USE APPROVED SAFETY EQUIPMENT . (a) Clean the circuit card and connectors with the soft bristle brush, STD-123, and alcohol, B50073. WARNING WHEN YOU USE THE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN, TO COOL OR TO DRY , DO NOT EXCEED 30 PSIG (207 KPA). MAKE SURE YOU WEAR EYE PROTECTION AND DO NOT DIRECT COMPRESSED AIR AT YOURSELF AND OTHERS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. (b) Dry the parts with the dry, clean air source 0 to 60 psi, STD-4107, at a maximum pressure of 30 psig (207 kPa). 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued)

G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 26-14-01-420-014 CAUTION BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE OR INSTALL THE SCREEN OF THE COVER ASSEMBLY AND LABYRINTH. THIS WILL HELP PREVENT SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH. SCRATCHES ON THE LABYRINTH CAN CAUSE STRAY LIGHT REFLECTIONS AND INCORRECT CALIBRATIONS TO OCCUR. CAUTION ESDS COMPONENTS AND UNITS MUST BE PROTECTED FROM STATIC DISCHARGE DAMAGE AT ALL TIMES. (1) Assemble the lavatory smoke detector as follows: (a) Install the fasteners [23], washers [24], and nuts [25] to attach the labyrinth top [26] to the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12]. 1) Tighten the nuts [25] to 4 in-lb (0.45 N·m) - 5 in-lb (0.56 N·m). (b) Connect the connector [27] to the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12]. (c) Install the screws [21] and washers [22] to attach the CCA and labyrinth assembly [12] to the housing/screen assembly [11]. 1) Tighten the screws [21] to 8 in-lb (0.90 N·m) - 9 in-lb (1.02 N·m). (d) Put together the cover [15] and EMI gasket [28]. 1) If disconnected, connect the bonding jumper [32] to the cover/EMI gasket assembly with the flat washer [29], lock washer [30], and nut [31]. a) Tighten the nut [31] to 8 in-lb (0.90 N·m) - 9 in-lb (1.02 N·m). (e) Install the gasket [20] on the connector [18]. (f) Put the connector [18] into the opening in the housing/screen assembly [11]. (g) Install the slide-lock-post [16] and washer [17] to attach the connector [18] to the housing/screen assembly [11]. NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one hex nut, one lock washer, one flat washer, and one slide-lock-post. 1) Make sure that the gasket [20] is between the connector [18] and the inner side of the housing/screen assembly [11]. (h) Install the slide-lock-post [16], washer [17], and bonding jumper [19] to attach the connector [18] to the housing/screen assembly [11]. NOTE: The slide-lock-post can include one washer, one hex nut, and one slide-lock-post. (i) Tighten the slide-lock-posts [16] to 5 in-lb (0.56 N·m) - 6 in-lb (0.68 N·m). (j) Install the screws [13] and washer [14] to attach the cover/EMI gasket assembly to the housing/screen assembly [11]. 1) Tighten the screws [13] to the 5 in-lb (0.56 N·m) - 6 in-lb (0.68 N·m). 26-14-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH KIDDE PHOTOELECTRIC DETECTORS (Continued) SUBTASK 26-14-01-400-001 (2) Do this task: Lavatory Smoke Detector Installation (Kidde), TASK 26-14-01-400-802.

Task Description

2. APU Fire Detection - Deactivation (Figure 201)

A. General

(1) This task will deactivate the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire detection system.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right
