26-15-00-710-801APU Fire Detection - Operational Test ATA 26 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. APU Fire Detection - Operational Test (Figure 501) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) The operational test does a check of the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire detection system without disturbing the detection system and it only uses the equipment that is on the airplane. This test will verify the circuit from the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, to the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, and to the P28 APU remote panel. The test will make sure that the engine and APU fire detection control module, that continuously monitors the detection loops, operates correctly. NOTE: If the APU fire detection system is inoperative, the FIRE WARN lights and ALARM BELL are not activated. Only if the engines and APU are functioning properly, the FIRE WARN lights and ALARM BELL will be activated. The circuit design precludes latent failure modes due to the parallel path of the test circuit and actuation circuit. NOTE: If the Alternating Current (AC) power is removed (no power on main buses) and if there is a faulty APU LOOP , the FIRE BELL, FIRE WARNING light, MASTER CAUTION, and OVHT/DET lights are not activated.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Prepare for the Operational Test

SUBTASK 26-15-00-860-001 (1) Make sure that the electrical power is supplied (TASK 24-22-00-860-811). SUBTASK 26-15-00-860-002 (2) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2 A 23 C00403 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION APU 26-15-00 (Continued) F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1 SUBTASK 26-15-00-860-003 (3) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 19 C00388 FIRE PROTECTION DET OVHT WW WING BODY

E. FIRE/OVHT Test

SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-001 (1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the OVHT/FIRE position. NOTE: When performing the Fire/OVHT test with a faulted APU fire detector loop, the MASTER CAUTION lights are off, the FIRE WARN and OVHT/DET lights are off, the fire bell does not sound and the APU DET INOP light will be on. NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the engine and wheel well detection system and the indications, on the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the electronic equipment bay. (a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the APU Fire Switch light (red) comes on. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on: 1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber). 2) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red). 3) The OVHT/DET light (amber). (c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, in the aural warning module, M315, comes on. (d) In the right main wheel well, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The APU remote fire light (red), on the P28 APU remote panel, flashes on and off continuously. 2) The APU fire warning horn comes on. SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-002 (2) Release the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel. (a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that the APU Fire Switch light (red) goes off. (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights goes off: 1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber). 2) The captain's and first officer's FIRE WARN lights (red). 3) The OVHT/DET light (amber). (c) Make sure that the flight compartment fire bell, in the aural warning module, M315, stops. (d) In the right main wheel well, make sure that these conditions occur: 1) The APU remote fire light (red), on the P28 APU remote panel, goes off. 26-15-00 2) The APU fire warning horn stops. F. FAULT/INOP Test from the P8-1 Engine and APU Fire Control Panel SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-003 (1) Move and hold the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, to the FAULT/INOP position. NOTE: Ignore the indication lights for the engine detection system and the indications, on the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the electronic equipment bay. (a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights comes on: 1) APU DET INOP light (amber). NOTE: When performing the FAULT/INOP Test with a faulted APU detector loop, the APU DET INOP light will stay on, the MASTER CAUTION lights are off and the FAULT light will come on during the test. 2) FAULT light (amber) (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights comes on: 1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber). 2) The OVHT/DET light (amber). SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-004 (2) Release the TEST switch, on the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel. (a) On the P8-1 engine and APU fire control panel, make sure that these lights are off: 1) APU DET INOP light (amber). 2) FAULT light (amber). (b) On the P7 glareshield panel, make sure that these lights are off: 1) The captain's and first officer's MASTER CAUTION lights (amber). 2) The OVHT/DET light (amber).

G. FAULT/INOP Test from the Engine and APU Fire Detection Control Module, M279

SUBTASK 26-15-00-710-005 (1) Push and hold the FAULT/INOP TEST switch, on the engine and APU fire detection control module, M279, in the electrical and electronics compartment. H. APU Overheat Detector Element Installation

SUBTASK 26-15-01-420-001 WARNING MAKE SURE THAT YOU STAY AWAY FROM ELEVATOR PARTS THAT MOVE. INTERNAL ELEMENTS FROM THE ELEVATOR SYSTEM CAN MOVE SUDDENLY IF THE AIRCRAFT IS OPEN TO WIND. DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. (1) Do these steps to install an overheat detector element [7]: (a) Make sure that the detector housing and mounting bracket are clean. (b) Put the overheat detector element [7] in position along the support tube [6]. (c) Put the overheat detector housing in the correct position on the mounting bracket. (d) Install the screws [2], washers [16], and nuts [17] that attach the overheat detector housing to the mounting bracket. 1) Tighten the nuts [17] to 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m) to 30 in-lb (3.4 N·m). (e) Install the grommets [8] on the detector element [7] at each clamp [9] location. (f) Install the element [7] in the clamp [9]. (g) Make sure that the end of the detector element [7] ends less than 1 in. (25 mm) past the last grommet [8]. 1) If the detector element [7] extends more than 1 in. (25 mm), adjust the length of the element between each clamp [9] so that any slack is evenly distributed. (h) Make sure that there is at least 0.12 in. (3.0 mm) between the overheat detector element [7] and support tube [6]. 26-15-01 CAUTION FOR THE LAST ASSEMBLY , USE LUBRICATED SCREWS. IF SCREWS ARE REUSED, OR IF NON-LUBRICATED SCREWS ARE USED THEY CAN FREEZE BECAUSE OF HIGH TEMPERATURES. THE THREADS ON THESE SCREWS MUST HAVE A LAYER OF COMPOUND, D00004, BEFORE INSTALLATION. (i) Apply compound, D00004, to the screw [10] threads. (j) Tighten the screw [10] on each clamp [9] until 1-1/2 to 3 threads extend through the nut. 1) Make sure that the grommets [8] and clamp [9] are tight. 2) Make sure that the ends of the screws [10] do not touch the support tube [6]. (k) Apply compound, D00004, to the threads of the terminal lug. (l) Put the lead in position on the terminal lug, and attach with a washer [4] and nut [5]. (m) Tighten the nut [5] to 32.0 in-lb (3.6 N·m) to 38.0 in-lb (4.3 N·m). (n) Apply Silcoset 152 adhesive, A50154, to the terminal lugs. SUBTASK 26-15-01-280-001 (2) Do a check of the resistance between the element stud and the airplane structure. (a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550, or equivalent, that can measure 0.0001 ohm (0.1 milliohm). (b) Make sure that the resistance is less than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms). (c) If the resistance is more than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms), clean the bonding surfaces with the solvent, B00083, and do the check again.

26-16-00-730-801Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Smoke Test ATA 26 · Task 730-801

Task Description

3. Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Smoke Test (Figure 501)

A. General

(1) When the DET SELECT switch is set to A, and any detector in loop A detects smoke, an alarm will occur. Similarly, when the DET SELECT switch is set to B, and the B loop detects smoke, the alarm will occur. When the DET SELECT switch is set to NORM, smoke must be detected by a detector in loop A and loop B (any detector in both loops, they do not have to be a pair) before the alarm will occur. NOTE: If you apply too much smoke to a detector, the excess smoke could affect adjacent detectors. Try to limit the amount of smoke applied to each detector.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-11202 Smoke Generator - Lavatory/Crew Rest Smoke Detector (or 737 Cargo Area Type Smoke Detectors) Part #: HO-25S Supplier: 61908 Part #: SOLOA10 Supplier: 1WU32 Opt Part #: SOLOA3 Supplier: 1WU32 Opt Part #: SOLOA4 Supplier: 1WU32

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left 122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right 141 Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 142 Aft Cargo Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Prepare for the Test

SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-005 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name C 16 C01523 CARGO FIRE FWD DET B C 17 C01522 CARGO FIRE FWD DET A C 18 C01525 CARGO FIRE AFT DET B C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A

E. Smoke Test

SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-006 (1) On the Cargo Fire Control panel P8, set the DET SELECT FWD switch to A. SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-007 (2) On the Cargo Fire Control pane P8, set the DET SELECT AFT switch to NORM. 26-16-00 SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-005 (3) Use a smoke generator, COM-11202, to make smoke adjacent to the forward left detector in the forward cargo bay. SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-006 (4) Make sure that these indications occur: (a) On the Cargo Bay Fire Control panel, the ARM FWD light (red) comes on. (b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights (red) come on. (c) In the flight compartment, the fire bell comes on. SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-003 (5) On the Cargo Fire Control panel, set the DET SELECT FWD and DET SELECT AFT switch to NORM. (a) Make sure all the alarm indications stop. SUBTASK 26-16-00-860-008 (6) Clear the smoke from the cargo bay. NOTE: If the smoke is not cleared from the cargo bay within 60 seconds, the smoke indications will occur again. SUBTASK 26-16-00-730-004 (7) Do the Smoke Test for each of the other cargo bay smoke detectors. (1) Before the smoke detector [2] is touched, do the procedure for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic discharge, as follows: • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802 26-16-01 • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802. SUBTASK 26-16-01-020-005 (2) For the forward cargo bay smoke detector, do these steps: (a) Remove the screws [3] that hold the guard assembly [1] to the cargo ceiling. Task Description

2. Cargo Bay Smoke Detector - Cleaning (Figure 701)

A. References

Reference Title 26-16-00-710-801 Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Operational Test (P/B 501)

Reference Description STD-123 Brush - Soft Bristle STD-1086 Gloves - Rubber STD-3939 Air Source - Regulated, Dry Filtered, 0 to 10 psig

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left 122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right 141 Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 142 Aft Cargo Compartment - Right

E. Cargo Bay Smoke Detector Cleaning

SUBTASK 26-16-01-860-001 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name C 16 C01523 CARGO FIRE FWD DET B C 17 C01522 CARGO FIRE FWD DET A C 18 C01525 CARGO FIRE AFT DET B C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A 26-16-01 SUBTASK 26-16-01-160-001 WARNING WHEN YOU CLEAN THE SMOKE DETECTOR AND SENSOR, MAKE SURE THAT THE AREA HAS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. KEEP THE ALCOHOL AWAY FROM HEAT , SPARKS OR FLAMES. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE ALCOHOL. PUT ON RUBBER GLOVES. ALCOHOL IS A FLAMMABLE AND POISONOUS SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . WARNING ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS AN AGENT THAT IS FLAMMABLE, EXPLOSIVE, AND POISONOUS. MAKE SURE ALL PERSONS OBEY ALL OF THE PRECAUTIONS WHEN ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IS USED. • GAS/AIR MIXTURES MORE THAN THE LOWER EXPLOSIVE LIMIT (LEL) CAN CAUSE AN EXPLOSION IF HIGH HEAT , SPARKS, OR FLAMES SUPPLY IGNITION. • USE IN AN AREA OPEN TO THE AIR. • CLOSE THE CONTAINER WHEN NOT USED. • DO NOT GET ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IN THE EYES, ON THE SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. • DO NOT BREATHE THE GAS. (2) Put on rubber glove, STD-1086. SUBTASK 26-16-01-160-002 (3) Remove the dirt from the exterior of the smoke detector with the cotton wiper, G00034, soaked with alcohol, B00065. SUBTASK 26-16-01-210-001 CAUTION BLOW THE AIR LIGHTLY INTO THE SENSOR ASSEMBLY . TOO MUCH FORCE CAN CAUSE STATIC ELECTRICITY ON THE ELECTRODES WHICH CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SMOKE SENSOR. (4) Use a soft bristle brush, STD-123, and compressed nitrogen at a maximum pressure of 15 psig, or with the 0 to 10 psig air source, STD-3939, lightly blow any dirt or particles out of the sensor assembly. NOTE: Scheduled cleaning is not necessary, but the smoke detector labyrinth must be kept clean to operate accurately. (a) Clean the connector. (b) Clean the dust screens. (c) Clean the inner area of the labyrinth. NOTE: Refer to the applicable CMM for the instructions. It is not necessary to fully disassemble the smoke detector to clean the inner area of the labyrinth. (5) Clean the electrical connector pins with alcohol, B50073. (6) Install the cover if removed. 26-16-01 SUBTASK 26-16-01-860-002 (7) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name C 16 C01523 CARGO FIRE FWD DET B C 17 C01522 CARGO FIRE FWD DET A C 18 C01525 CARGO FIRE AFT DET B C 19 C01524 CARGO FIRE AFT DET A SUBTASK 26-16-01-710-003 (8) Do this task: Cargo Bay Smoke Detection - Operational Test, TASK 26-16-00-710-801.

26-18-00-710-801Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection System - Operational Test ATA 26 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Detection System - Operational Test (Figure 501) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) This procedure does a check of the Overheat Fire Detection System without disturbing the detection system and it only uses the equipment that is on the airplane. (2) This procedure can be performed on the flight deck, or in the Electrical / Electronic (E/E) bay.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Prepare for the Operational Test

SUBTASK 26-18-00-860-002 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 19 C00388 FIRE PROTECTION DET OVHT WW WING BODY A 21 C00396 FIRE PROT DETECTION MA WRN & CONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 12 C00132 MASTER CAUTION ANNUNCIATOR BUS 1 B 13 C00131 MASTER CAUTION ANNUNCIATOR BAT D 11 C00133 INDICATOR MASTER DIM DIM/TST CONT D 12 C00310 INDICATOR MASTER DIM BAT D 13 C00311 INDICATOR MASTER DIM BUS 1 D 14 C00312 INDICATOR MASTER DIM BUS 2 E 11 C00313 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 1 E 12 C00314 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 2 E 13 C00315 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 3 E 14 C00316 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 4 F 11 C00317 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 5 26-18-00 (Continued) F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name F 12 C00318 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 6 F 13 C01179 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 7 F 14 C01180 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 8 SUBTASK 26-18-00-860-003 (2) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2 A 23 C00403 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION APU A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1

D. Overheat Detection System Operational Test - Flight Compartment

SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-001 (1) Move and hold the TEST switch on the Engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) Fire Control Panel to the OVHT/FIRE position. (a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel comes on. (b) Make sure the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights on the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, come on. (c) Make sure the flight compartment fire bell in the Aural Warning Module, M315, comes on. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-013 (2) Push the BELL CUTOUT switch on the P8-1 panel. (a) Make sure the fire bell goes off immediately. (b) Make sure that both FIRE WARN lights go off. (c) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the P8-1 panel remains on. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-014 (3) Set the LIGHTS switch on the pilots main panel, P1 to DIM. (a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light dims. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-015 (4) Set the LIGHTS switch on the pilots main panel, P1 to BRT . (a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light returns to its original brightness. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-002 (5) Release the TEST switch on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel. (a) Make sure the WHEEL WELL light on the Engine and APU Fire Control Panel goes off. (b) Make sure the Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN lights on the Pilot's Glareshield remains off. (c) Make sure the flight compartment fire bell in the Aural Warning Module, M315, remains off. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-003 (6) Push and hold the OVHT TEST switch on the air conditioning panel, P5-10, for at least 5 seconds. AIRCRAFT MAINTENANCE MANUAL 26-18-00 (a) On the air conditioning panel, P5-10, make sure these lights come on: 1) The left WING-BODY OVERHEAT light. 2) The right WING-BODY OVERHEAT light. (b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure these lights come on: 1) The AIR COND light. 2) The Captain's and First Officer's MASTER CAUTION lights. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-004 (7) Release the OVHT TEST switch on the air conditioning panel, P5-10. (a) On the air conditioning panel, P5-10, make sure these lights go off: 1) The left WING-BODY OVERHEAT light. 2) The right WING-BODY OVERHEAT light. (b) On the Pilot's Glareshield, P7, make sure these lights go off: 1) The AIR COND light. 2) The Captain's and First Officer's MASTER CAUTION lights. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300

E. Overheat Detection System Operational Test - E/E Bay

SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-005 (1) Push and release the DISP TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module, M237. (a) Make sure the code 88 comes on, then goes off. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-006 (2) Push and release the LOC TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module, M237. (a) Make sure the code 90 comes on while the test is in progress, then the code 99 comes on to show there are no faults. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-007 (3) Push and release the MEM READ switch on the compartment overheat detection module, M237. (a) Make sure the code 97 comes on to show the test is complete. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-008 (4) Push and release the MEM CLEAR switch on the compartment overheat detection module, M237. (a) Make sure the code 97 goes off. (b) Make sure the MAINT ADV light stays off. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 F. Overheat Detection System Operational Test - E/E Bay SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-009 (1) Push and release the DISPLAY TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. (a) Make sure all Light Emitting Diode (LED)s come on, then go off. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-010 (2) Push and release the LOCAL TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 26-18-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (Continued) (a) Make sure the code CONFIG NEXTGEN1 comes on while the test is in progress, then the code TEST COMPLETE comes on to show there are no faults. NOTE: If other codes are displayed, faults exist in the system which must be corrected. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-011 (3) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch on the compartment overheat detection module. (a) Make sure the code END OF MEMORY comes on to show the MEMORY READ is complete. SUBTASK 26-18-00-710-012 (4) Push and hold the MEMORY CLEAR switch on the compartment overheat detection module, M237. (a) When MEMORY CLEAR displays, release the switch. NOTE: This can be approximately 5 seconds. (b) Make sure the MAINT ADV light stays off. RYR ALL

G. Put the Airplane Back to its Usual Condition.

SUBTASK 26-18-00-860-004 (1) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 22 C00407 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 2 A 23 C00403 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION APU A 24 C00405 FIRE PROTECTION DETECTION ENG 1

A. General

(1) The wheel well fire, wing and body overheat detection control module, M237 is located on the E1-4 shelf in the Electronic Equipment Compartment (EEC). The wheel well fire, wing & body overheat detection control module will be referred to as the control module throughout this procedure. Access the front of panel of the control module to do the Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) test. (2) This task gives instructions to perform these procedures with the control module: (a) Display Test Procedure (b) Local Test Procedure (c) Memory Read Procedure (d) Memory Clear Procedure RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (e) Zone Resistance Test. RYR ALL (3) Do not hold the control module switches in the CLOSED position for more than 10 seconds or the software could stop the test. (4) Unless the test specifies differently, continue from one step to the subsequent step in less than 40 seconds.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left

C. BITE Procedure

SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-001 (1) Do these steps to do the display procedure: RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300 (a) Push and release the DISP TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) Make sure the code 88 comes on, then goes off. 26-18-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (b) Push and release the DISPLAY TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) Make sure all Light Emitting Diode (LED)s come on, then go off. RYR ALL SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-002 (2) Do these steps to do the local test procedure for the control module: RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300 (a) Push and release the LOC TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) Make sure the FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code 90 comes on while the test is in progress, then the code 99 comes on to show the test is complete. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (b) Push and release the LOCAL TEST switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) Make sure the FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code CONFIG NEXTGEN1 comes on while the self-test is in progress, then the code TEST COMPLETE comes on to show there are no faults. NOTE: If other codes are displayed, faults exist in the system which must be corrected. RYR ALL (c) If an alarm/fault condition is found, the display will show a fault code. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300 1) Refer to the table at the end of this task to find the fault isolation task for the applicable maintenance message for the fault indicated by the FAULT & ALARM CODE display. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 2) Refer to the table at the end of this task to find the fault isolation task for the applicable maintenance message for the fault indicated by the FAULT & ALARM CODES display. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300 SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-003 (3) Do these steps to do the memory read procedure: (a) Push the MEM READ switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows the latest fault code. NOTE: As many as 10 alarm/fault codes can be kept in non-volatile memory. Code 97 shows that all codes have been read. (b) Push the MEM READ switch again until all the alarm/fault codes have been read, and code 97 shows. (c) Push the MEM READ switch again so the display shows no data. 26-18-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-004 (4) Do these steps to do the memory read procedure: (a) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch on the compartment overheat detection module. 1) Make sure the code END OF MEMORY comes on to show the test is complete. 2) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again until all the alarm/faults have been read. NOTE: A minimum of 10 Line Replaceable Unit (LRU) failures can be stored in non-volatile memory. 3) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again so the display shows no data. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH 2 DIGIT CODM: P/N 35008-307 OR P/N 20-035008-300 SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-005 (5) Do these steps to do the memory clear procedure. (a) Push the DISP TEST switch on the control module. 1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows code 88 and then the display is blank. (b) Push the LOC TEST switch on the control module. 1) The FAULT & ALARM CODE display shows 90, then 99. NOTE: After code 99 is shown, continue with the next step in less than 10 seconds. 2) If code 93 or 94 is displayed, push and release LOC TEST until code 99 is shown. NOTE: After code 99 is shown, continue with the next step in less than 10 seconds. (c) Push and release the MEM READ switch on the control module until the display shows the code to be removed. NOTE: If there are fault codes in the memory, the display will show the last code that was recorded. If there are no fault codes in the memory, the display will show code 97. (d) Push and release the MEM CLEAR switch to clear the displayed code. 1) The display shows no data. (e) Push and release the MEM READ switch again to display the next code. 1) Repeat the two steps above until code 97 appears. NOTE: Alarm/fault codes can not be removed until the condition is corrected. (f) Push the MEM READ switch again so the display shows no data and the MAINT ADV light is off. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-006 (6) Do these steps to do the memory clear procedure. (a) Push and hold the MEMORY CLEAR switch on the control module. (b) When MEMORY CLEAR displays, release the switch. NOTE: This can be approximately 5 seconds. 26-18-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (Continued) (c) Make sure that the MAINT ADV light stays off. NOTE: If there are fault codes in the memory, the display will show the last code that was recorded. If there are no fault codes in the memory, the display will show no data. (d) Push and release the MEMORY CLEAR switch to clear the displayed code. 1) The display shows no data. (e) Push and release the MEMORY READ switch again to display the next code. 1) Repeat the two steps above until no data shows. NOTE: Alarm/fault codes can not be removed until the condition is corrected. (f) Push the MEMORY READ switch again so the display shows no data and the MAINT ADV light is off. SUBTASK 26-18-01-740-007 (7) Do these steps to do the Zone Resistance test (Compartment Overheat Detection Module (CODM) BITE): (a) Push and release the LOCAL TEST button on the front panel of the CODM. 1) The front panel of the CODM displays CONFIG NEXTGEN1 followed by L WING. (b) Continue to push and release LOCAL TEST . This will cycle through the different zones followed by LOOP ___ which shows the resistance value in the loop or 2GND_____ which shows the resistance value to ground. NOTE: If the loops are functioning properly all zones will have a 2GND value of 5000. (c) Make sure that the resistance value agree with the Table 501 below: Table 501/26-18-01-993-801 Zone Resistance Test Data KIDDE ALARM/FAULT CODES RESISTANCE VALUE or FAULT MESSAGE SENSORS L WING L WING OPEN L WING OVHT L WING SHRT < 15 Ohms OPEN LOW 0 M370, M268, M1761, M1762 L PACK BAY L PACK BAY OPEN L PACK BAY OVHT L PACK BAY SHRT < 15 Ohms OPEN LOW 0 M1911, M1909, M355 KEELBEAM KEELBEAM OPEN KEELBEAM OVHT KEELBEAM SHRT < 15 Ohms OPEN LOW 0 M272, M273, M3922 R SIDE R SIDE OPEN R SIDE OVHT R SIDE SHRT < 20 Ohms OPEN LOW 0 M1763, M1764, M269, M371, M356, M1910, M1912 26-18-01 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH CODM P/N 475928-01 (Continued) Table 501/26-18-01-993-801 Zone Resistance Test Data (Continued) KIDDE ALARM/FAULT CODES RESISTANCE VALUE or FAULT MESSAGE SENSORS MWW LOOP A MWW LOOP A OPEN MWW LOOP A FIRE MWW LOOP A SHRT < 15 Ohms OPEN LOW 0 M270 RYR ALL

E. Prepare for the Compartment Overheat System Automated Test

SUBTASK 26-18-01-860-006 (1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. NOTE: Failure to remove power from the aircraft will result in inaccurate test results. SUBTASK 26-18-01-020-003 (2) Do this task: Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit Removal, TASK 26-18-01-000-801. SUBTASK 26-18-01-480-001 (3) Install the aero fli overheat detection loop tester, SPL-23026: (a) Install the interface module on the Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit rack. (b) Connect the cable to the interface tablet and the interface module. (c) Turn on the interface module. (d) Turn on the interface tablet. F. Compartment Overheat System Automated Test SUBTASK 26-18-01-700-001 (1) Perform the automated test: NOTE: For detailed instructions please refer to equipment user manual. (a) Start the AERO FLi (or OHDL Studio) App. 26-18-01 (b) Enter the applicable Operator, Station, and Aircraft ID information. NOTE: This will be used for PDF report. (c) Select the applicable Program. (d) Select Loop: [All]. (e) Select Static Test. (f) Select the applicable loops that are to be scanned. (g) it's Select Launch Scan. 1) If the scan takes longer than two minutes, restart the hardware. (h) When scan is finished, select PDF Report. (i) Review the PDF report to identify possible problems among the reported items. 1) If problems are found, the report will show the location of the problem. 2) Correct the problems and perform the automated test again. 3) When the problems have been corrected, there will be no problems listed in the report.

G. Put the Aircraft Back to its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 26-18-01-080-001 (1) Remove the Aero FLi interface module: (a) Turn off the interface module. (b) Turn off the interface tablet. (c) Disconnect the interface cable. (d) Remove the module from the rack. SUBTASK 26-18-01-020-004 (2) Do this task: Compartment Overheat Detection Control Unit Installation, TASK 26-18-01-400-801. SUBTASK 26-18-01-860-007 (3) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811.

(b) For the forward strut locations, remove the bolts [38] and washers [39]. (c) For the aft strut locations, remove the screws [44] and washers [45]. (a) Open the clamp [35] to free the element. (b) Remove the grommet [5] from the element. NOTE: Keep the grommet for the installation procedure. 26-18-02-400-801Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Sensor Element - Installation ATA 26 · Task 400-801

Task Description

3. Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Sensor Element - Installation (Figure 401, Figure 402, Figure 403, Figure 404, Figure 406, Figure 407, Figure 408, Figure 409)

A. General

(1) This task gives instructions to install the Wheel Well, Wing and Lower Aft Body Overheat Sensor Element.

I. Long Sensor Element - Installation

NOTE: This procedure only applies to the following sensors: element [15], M356, R FWD A/C PACK element [16], M370, L WING INBOARD element [16], M371, R WING INBOARD element [17], M268, L WING OUTBOARD element [17], M269, R WING OUTBOARD element [19], M276, AFT - SECTION 48 element [20], M275, AFT - SECTION 47 element [21], M347, AFT - SECTION 46 element [22], M1147, AFT - SECTION 46 element [23], M348, AFT - SECTION 46 element [24], M273, AFT KEELBEAM element [25], M272, FWD KEELBEAM element [28], M355, L FWD A/C PACK 26-18-02 element [29], M270, WHEEL WELL. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-001 CAUTION WHEN YOU INSTALL THE ELEMENT , DO NOT BEND THE ELEMENT TO A RADIUS THAT IS LESS THAN 1 IN. (25.4 MM.). DO NOT LET A BEND IN 1 IN. (25.4 MM.) DISTANCE FROM THE EDGE OF THE QUICK-RELEASE CLAMP OR THE FLANGE ASSEMBLY . CONTOUR THE ELEMENT TO CLEAR ADJACENT PARTS AND STRUCTURES. HAVE THE ELEMENT LENGTH EQUALLY BETWEEN CLAMPS. IF THE BEND RADIUS IS LESS THAN ONE INCH, DAMAGE TO THE ELEMENT CAN OCCUR. CAUTION DO NOT TURN, PULL, OR ATTACH THE SENSING ELEMENT WITH A CLAMP . DO NOT MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT . IF YOU MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT , DAMAGE TO THE SENSING ELEMENT CAN OCCUR. (1) Carefully put the element into position. NOTE: This step will require two people. (a) Carefully unwind the element and make sure that there are no kinks. (b) Carefully bend the element to fit into its approximate position. (c) Carefully put and hold the element in its position. (d) Do these steps for the sensor elements on the aft keel beam: 1) Tie the existing cord to the aft end of the new element. 2) Slowly pull the forward through the keel beam. a) Use the access holes below the keel beam to help guide the element through the holes in the structure. 3) Remove the cord from the element. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-024 (2) Remove the caps from the end fittings of the electrical connectors [3]. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-025 WARNING DO THE STEPS BELOW IF THE AIRPLANE OPERATES WHERE DEICING FLUID THAT CONTAINS POTASSIUM FORMATE OR POTASSIUM ACETATE IS USED. ALSO, DO THE STEPS FOR ALL AIRPLANES THAT YOU FOUND CORROSION IN THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTORS IN THE MAIN WHEEL WELL. THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTORS ARE IN A SYSTEM THAT IS NECESSARY FOR SAFE FLIGHT . (3) Examine the connector [3] at each end of the heat detector for corrosion. (a) If corrosion is present, replace the element. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-026 (4) Put the covers on the open end of the connectors [3] with caps to prevent damage. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-011 (5) Install the element into each of the mounting clips [4]. (a) Install the grommet [5] around the element so that each grommet [5] is in the center of the mounting clip [4]. 26-18-02 (b) Install the grommets [5] into the mounting clips [4] so that the grommet slit is at 90±20 degrees from the clamp opening and hinge centerline except at bends where the slit is facing outside of the nearest bend (Figure 409). 1) Make sure that the grommet slit is visible and not facing adjacent structure. 2) Make sure that the element does not contact the mounting clips [4]. 3) Make sure that the mounting clips [4] and grommets [5] are attached to the element tightly. a) If the used sensor element is in its position as a reference, remove it. (c) Locate element in approximate installation position and temporarily support as necessary to maintain position. (d) Tighten the bolt [6] to secure the mounting clip [4]. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020 (e) For the Wing-to-body zone, make sure that the minimum clearance is as follows: 1) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the mounting clips [4]. 2) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the mounting clips [4]. 3) 1.65 in. (41.91 mm) between the sensor element and duct. (f) For the Wing Leading Edge zone, make sure that the minimum clearance is as follows: 1) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the mounting clips [4]. 2) 0.15 in. (3.81 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the mounting clips [4]. 3) 1.5 in. (38.10 mm) between the sensor element and duct. RYR 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132 (g) For the Bulkhead zone, make sure that the minimum clearance is as follows: 1) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the mounting clips [4]. 2) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the mounting clips [4]. 3) 1.5 in. (38.10 mm) between the sensor element and duct. RYR ALL (h) For the other zones, make sure that the minimum clearance is as follows: RYR 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-516 1) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the mounting clips [4]. 2) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the mounting clips [4]. 3) 1.5 in. (38.10 mm) between the sensor element and duct. 26-18-02 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 517-999 4) 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) between the sensor element and structure, except at the mounting clips [4]. 5) 0.50 in. (12.70 mm) between the sensor element and other systems, except at the mounting clips [4]. 6) 3 in. (76.20 mm) between the element and duct. RYR ALL (i) Make sure that the minimum clearance between the sensor elements is 0.50 in. (12.70 mm). SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-002 (6) Remove the caps from the end fittings to connect the connectors [3]. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-022 (7) Make sure that the connectors [3] have ceramic inserts on both ends. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-023 (8) If it is applicable, make sure that the pins on the connectors [3] are centered, not bent, recessed, or damaged. SUBTASK 26-18-02-100-002 (9) Clean the mating surfaces of the connectors [3], element, and structure (SWPM 20-20-00). (a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130. (b) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-094 PRE SB 737-26-1124 SUBTASK 26-18-02-390-001 (10) For the M270 sensor, apply Compound, G50470, to the internal components of the connectors [3] and the ends of the element (SWPM 20-60-03). (1.57 mm) diameter. 1) Install a MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347, on the jamnut [2] and connector [3]. 2) If it is necessary, install a MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347, on the jamnut [2] and coupling element connector or electrical connector. 3) Obtain adequate tension without overstressing the lockwire [1] (MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347), apply the number of twists per inch of lockwire as in Table 402. 26-18-02 Table 402/26-18-02-993-809 TWISTS PER INCH RANGE OF WIRE DIAMETERS (IN) LESS THAN 0.019 0.019 TO 0.026 0.027 TO 0.042 0.043 TO 0.065 MORE THAN 0.065 Twists per inch 11 to 14 9 to 12 7 to 10 5 to 8 4 to 7 SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-029 (12) For each sensor element in the main wheel well, do these steps to connect the connector [3]. J. Looped Sensor Element - Installation

NOTE: This procedure only applies to the following sensors: element [13], M1912, R AFT A/C PACK element [14], M1910, R MID A/C PACK element [18], M1763, R FWD STRUT element [18], M1761, L STRUT FWD element [30], M1762, L STRUT AFT element [30], M1764, R AFT STRUT element [26], M1911, L AFT A/C PACK element [27], M1909, L MID A/C PACK. SUBTASK 26-18-02-100-001 (1) Clean the mating surfaces of the bracket [12] and element (SWPM 20-20-00). (a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130. (b) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper. SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-005 CAUTION WHEN YOU INSTALL THE ELEMENT , DO NOT BEND THE ELEMENT TO A RADIUS THAT IS LESS THAN 1 IN. (25.4 MM.). DO NOT LET A BEND IN 1 IN. (25.4 MM.) DISTANCE FROM THE EDGE OF THE QUICK-RELEASE CLAMP OR THE FLANGE ASSEMBLY . CONTOUR THE ELEMENT TO CLEAR ADJACENT PARTS AND STRUCTURES. HAVE THE ELEMENT LENGTH EQUALLY BETWEEN CLAMPS. IF THE BEND RADIUS IS LESS THAN ONE INCH, DAMAGE TO THE ELEMENT CAN OCCUR. CAUTION DO NOT TURN, PULL, OR ATTACH THE SENSING ELEMENT WITH A CLAMP . DO NOT MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT . IF YOU MAKE SERVICEABLE KINKS, BENDS, OR DENTS STRAIGHT , DAMAGE TO THE SENSING ELEMENT CAN OCCUR. (2) Position the element in the bracket [12] and clamps [35]. NOTE: Make sure that the element is routed straight inside the clamps and minimum clearance is maintained as follow: 0.25 inches between the detector and adjacent structure or other systems; 1.5 inches between the detector and ducts. SUBTASK 26-18-02-390-003 (3) For M1762 and M1764, apply a fay seal between the bracket and sensors with sealant, A50359, sealant, A50337, or sealant, A50231 (SWPM 20-20-00). 26-18-02 SUBTASK 26-18-02-420-006 (4) Secure each terminal lug [10] with jamnut [2]. (a) Do this step for the sensor elements M1909, M1910, M1911, M1912, M1762, and M1764: 1) Tighten the jamnut [2] to 80 in-lb (9.0 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11.3 N·m). (b) Do this step for the sensor elements M1761 and M1763: 1) Tighten the jamnut [2] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 60 in-lb (6.8 N·m). (c) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the detector element sensors and bracket for M1909, M1910, M1911, M1912, M1762, and M1764 sensors (TASK 20-40-11-760-801). 1) Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614. 2) Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.005 ohms (5 milliohms) or less. (d) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the detector element sensors and bracket for M1761 and M1763 sensors (TASK 20-40-11-760-801). 1) Use a non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614. 2) Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.010 ohms (10 milliohms) or less. (e) Install a MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347 on the jamnuts [2], do this task: Lockwire, Cotter Pins, and Lockrings - Installation, TASK 20-10-44-400-801. (b) Make sure that the grommet slit is visible and not facing adjacent structure. (c) Make sure that the element does not contact the mounting clip. (d) Secure the clamp [35]. 26-18-02-720-803Wing, Wheel Well and Lower Aft Body - Overheat Sensing Element Test ATA 26 · Task 720-803

Task Description

2. Wing, Wheel Well and Lower Aft Body - Overheat Sensing Element Test

A. General

(1) Do a test of each sensing loop only when all of the units in the system are stable at ambient temperature. If you cannot complete the tests that follow on an element, replace the element.

26-20-00-210-802Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check ATA 26 · Task 210-802

Task Description

2. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check (Figure 601, Figure 602) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. References

Reference Title 26-21-01 P/B 401 ENGINE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION

B. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description STD-1336 Thermometer - Digital, 0-150 +/- 2 Degrees F , with a Probe, Thermocouple or Equivalent

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left

D. Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check

NOTE: This task is for fire extinguishing bottles with pressure gauges. No action is required if a pressure gauge is not installed. SUBTASK 26-20-00-860-002 (1) Allow the fire bottles to reach ambient temperature. NOTE: It may take several hours with the airplanes on the ground for the halon in the bottles to reach ambient temperature. SUBTASK 26-20-00-010-003 (2) Get access to the engine fire bottles that are located on the aft bulkhead in the left main landing gear wheel well. SUBTASK 26-20-00-970-002 WARNING KEEP MERCURY THERMOMETERS AWAY FROM THE AIRPLANE. DO NOT GET MERCURY IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. IF THE THERMOMETER BREAKS, MERCURY CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (3) Use a 0-150 +/- 2 degrees F digital thermometer, STD-1336, to get the ambient air temperature by the fire bottle. SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-003 (4) Find the acceptable pressure range for the measured ambient air temperature in the temperature curve (Figure 602). 26-20-00 SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-004 (5) Read the pressure gauge on the fire bottles. (a) Make sure that the pressure on the gauge is within the acceptable pressure range. 1) If the pressure on the gauge is not within the acceptable pressure range, replace the fire bottle with a serviceable fire bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-21-01/401).

SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-001 (4) Find the acceptable pressure range for the current temperature in the temperature curve (Figure 604). 26-20-00 SUBTASK 26-20-00-210-002 (5) Read the pressure gauge on the fire bottle. (a) If the pressure on the gauge is not within the limits shown, replace the fire bottle with a serviceable fire bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-22-01/401).

The primary circuit (A) is between pins 4 and 3; the alternate circuit (B) is between pins 6 and 7. Either circuit can fire the squib. There are different adapter cables in the test set to test each circuit.

B. References

Engine Fire Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test SUBTASK 26-21-00-010-001 WARNING DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL PANELS. SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-001 (3) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the L fire bottle discharge line at position 2. NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa). (a) When connecting the test equipment to the discharge lines, do not let the test equipment hang from the discharge lines. (b) Give sufficient support to the test equipment during the flow test. (c) Make sure that air flows through all the nozzles on the right engine. (d) Make sure that little or no air flows through the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4. SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-002 (4) Remove the pressure source from the L fire bottle discharge line at position 2. SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-003 (6) Remove the pressure source from the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4. SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-003 (7) Connect a pressure source, STD-1325, to the L fire bottle discharge line at position 1. NOTE: The pressure source, STD-1325, can supply 35 ±5 psig (241 ±35 kPa). SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-005 (10) Remove the pressure source from the R fire bottle discharge line at position 3. SUBTASK 26-21-00-420-004 (11) Connect the discharge tubes to the discharge outlets on both bottles positions 1 through 4. 26-21-00 WARNING DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head. 1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location. (b) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head. NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to engine number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to engine number 2. (c) Remove any unwanted compound. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (d) At positions 1 and 3, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb (50.8 ±2.6 N·m). CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (e) At positions 2 and 4, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 360 ±18 in-lb (40.7 ±2.0 N·m). SUBTASK 26-21-00-010-002 WARNING OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS WHEN YOU CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS, INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (12) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.

(a) Wait at least 3 minutes for the air pressure to stabilize. SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-002 (7) Close the pressure regulator. SUBTASK 26-21-00-820-001 (8) Turn off the pressure source. (b) Wait 2 to 3 minutes, then check the pressure. 1) Make sure that the pressure loss during the 2 to 3 minutes waiting period is not more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa). 2) If the pressure loss is more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa), do a check of the pressure source connections and the plugs used to seal the fire extinguishing nozzles. 3) If they do not show any indication of leakage, do a check of all fittings, seal surfaces, valves, and tubing in the discharge line. a) Repair the problems that you find. SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-003 (9) Bleed the fire extinguishing plumbing to 0 psi (0 kPa) and disconnect the test equipment from position 1. SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-008 (10) Install plugs on the fire extinguishing nozzles at the right engine (engine 2). NOTE: The equipment, SPL-1647, or other suitable plug can be used. SUBTASK 26-21-00-020-009 (11) Disconnect the tee fitting from the discharge outlet on the R fire bottle at position 4. SUBTASK 26-21-00-420-014 (12) Install protective cap on the fire bottle discharge outlet. SUBTASK 26-21-00-480-007 (13) Connect the test equipment to the R fire bottle discharge line at position 4. 26-21-00 SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-004 (14) Pressurize the discharge tubing that goes from R bottle to engine 2 to a pressure of 35 ±5 psi (241.3 ±34.5 kPa). (a) Wait at least 3 minutes for the air pressure to stabilize. SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-005 (15) Close the pressure regulator. SUBTASK 26-21-00-820-002 (16) Turn off the pressure source. (a) Record the stabilized air pressure. (b) Wait 2 to 3 minutes, then check the pressure. 1) Make sure that the pressure loss during the 2 to 3 minutes waiting period is not more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa). 2) If the pressure loss is more than 1 psi (6.9 kPa), do a check of the pressure source connections and the plugs used to seal the fire extinguishing nozzles. 3) If they do not show any indication of leakage, do a check of all fittings, seal surfaces, valves, and tubing in the discharge line. a) Repair the problems that you find. SUBTASK 26-21-00-780-006 (17) Bleed the fire extinguishing plumbing to 0 psi (0 kPa) and disconnect the pressure source at position 4. SUBTASK 26-21-00-020-014 CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT YOU REMOVE THE PLUGS FROM THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING NOZZLES AT THE TWO ENGINES. IF YOU DO NOT REMOVE THE PLUGS, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (18) Remove plugs on the fire extinguishing nozzles at the left engine (engine 1). SUBTASK 26-21-00-020-015 CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT YOU REMOVE THE PLUGS FROM THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING NOZZLES AT THE TWO ENGINES. IF YOU DO NOT REMOVE THE PLUGS, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (19) Remove plugs on the fire extinguishing nozzles at the right engine (engine 2). SUBTASK 26-21-00-080-007 (20) Remove the test equipment. SUBTASK 26-21-00-420-005 (21) Remove protective caps from the fire bottle discharge outlets. SUBTASK 26-21-00-420-015 (22) Connect the discharge tubes to the discharge outlets at positions 1 and 4. 26-21-00 WARNING DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head. 1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location. (b) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head. NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing that supplies extinguishant to engine number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing that supplies extinguishant to engine number 2. (c) Remove any unwanted compound. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (d) At the position 1, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb (50.8 ±2.6 N·m). CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (e) At the position 4, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 360 ±18 in-lb (40.7 ±2.0 N·m). (f) Make sure that there is 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) minimum clearance between the discharge tubes and electrical harness. SUBTASK 26-21-00-010-004 WARNING OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS WHEN YOU CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS, INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (23) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.

26-21-00-720-802Engine Fire Switch System Shutdown Test ATA 26 · Task 720-802

Task Description

6. Engine Fire Switch System Shutdown Test (Figure 501, Figure 505, Figure 506) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) This procedure checks that the engine is isolated from the fuel, hydraulic, pneumatic and electrical systems, and that the thrust reverser is disabled when the fire handle is pulled. It should be performed when the engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) fire control panel is replaced. (2) This test is the same for both engines.

B. References

Reference Title 29-11-00-860-801 Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization (P/B 201) 29-11-00-860-805 Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal (P/B 201) 78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201) 78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

G. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation

SUBTASK 26-21-01-940-004 WARNING MAKE SURE THAT THE DOWNLOCK PINS ARE INSTALLED IN ALL OF THE LANDING GEAR. WITHOUT THE DOWNLOCK PINS, THE LANDING GEAR CAN RETRACT AND CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (1) Make sure that the downlock pins are installed in the nose and main landing gear (PAGEBLOCK 32-00-01/201). SUBTASK 26-21-01-910-001 (2) Make sure that the airplane is grounded correctly (TASK 20-40-11-910-801). SUBTASK 26-21-01-280-001 (3) Use a force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [1]. SUBTASK 26-21-01-410-001 (4) If the protective caps [8] are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as follows: (a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] is 0.00 ±0.10 lb (0.00 ±0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate. 1) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as "Value A". 2) Make sure that protective caps [8] are installed on the squibs [7] and remove the rest of protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [1], before weighing. NOTE: Only the weight of the protective caps located on the squibs, supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle, are included on the weight listed on the ID plate. 3) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7], protective caps [8] installed on the squibs and record as "Value B". 4) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] ("Value A") and measured weight ("Value B") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace the fire extinguishing bottle [1]. SUBTASK 26-21-01-960-001 (5) If the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as follows: (a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] is 0.00 ±0.10 lb (0.00 ±0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate. 1) Weigh the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, including its caps and record as "Value A". 2) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] as "Value B". 3) Remove the rest of the protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [1], before weighing. 26-21-01 4) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7], shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168 (including shorting plugs' caps), and the protective caps [8] supplied for the squibs [7] and record as "Value C". NOTE: Weigh of protective caps supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle for the squibs only are included on ID plate. 5) Find the measured weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [1] by subtracting "Value A" from "Value C" and record as "Value D" (Value D = Value C - Value A). 6) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [1] ("Value B") and measured weight ("Value D") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace the fire extinguishing bottle [1]. SUBTASK 26-21-01-200-001 (6) If the previous fire extinguishing bottle [1] was discharged, do this step: (a) Check the discharge tube for any debris. NOTE: Pay particular attention to the discharge outlets at the engine. SUBTASK 26-21-01-200-002 (7) Examine the check valve to make sure that the ball moves freely. NOTE: It may be necessary to further disconnect the discharge tubes to get access to the check valve. SUBTASK 26-21-01-860-002 (8) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-001 (9) Do these steps to install the fire extinguishing bottle [1]: WARNING REMOVE THE CAPS FROM THE FILL FITTING ASSEMBLY ON THE FIRE EXTINGUISHER BOTTLES. THE SAFETY RELEASE ON THE BOTTLE WILL NOT OPERATE IF THE CAPS ARE ON. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , INJURY CAN OCCUR BECAUSE OF FIRE. (a) Remove the caps from the fill fitting assembly on the fire extinguishing bottle [1]. (b) Make sure that the inboard mounting lugs and support fixture mounting surfaces are clean. 1) Sand the support fixture mounting surfaces with 60 grit or finer wet/dry aluminum oxide sandpaper, G51464, or 180 grit or finer silicon carbide abrasive, G51924, or garnet 240 grit (7/0) or finer sandpaper, G51465. 2) Use cotton wiper, G00034, moist in solvent, B00184, to clean the washers [3], inboard mounting lugs, and mounting surfaces of the support fixture. (c) Apply a fay surface seal with sealant, A50110, sealant, A50155, sealant, A50231, or sealant, A50296, to the inboard mounting surfaces. 26-21-01 WARNING MAKE SURE THAT THE SHORTING PLUGS STAY CONNECTED WHEN THE TESTER COM-1550 OR A NON-FAIL-SAFE IGNITER TESTER IS IN OPERATION. AN ELECTRICAL CHARGE BETWEEN THE BOTTLE WALL AND INITIATOR PIN CAN CAUSE THE BOTTLE TO DEPLOY . IF THE BOTTLE DEPLOYS, INJURY TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU MOVE THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE. THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE IS HIGHLY PRESSURIZED AND HAS AN EXPLOSIVE SQUIB AS A COMPONENT . ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (d) Put the fire extinguishing bottle [1] in its correct position on the support bracket. (e) Install the bolts [2] and washers [3]. 1) If it is necessary, install two washers [3] between each mounting lug and support bracket. (f) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the fire extinguishing bottle [1] and support bracket (SWPM 20-20-00). 1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. 2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms) or less. a) If the resistance is more than 0.0025 ohm (2.5 milliohms), clean the bonding surfaces between the inboard mounting lugs and support bracket with solvent, B00083, and retest. (g) If the discharge head does not align with the hose or tube assembly, do this step: 1) Loosen the discharge outlet gland nut and rotate the discharge outlet so it aligns with the tube assembly. NOTE: Even if the discharge outlet is already aligned, the gland nut is not yet torqued down. (h) Tighten the gland nut to 540 in-lb (61.0 N·m) - 660 in-lb (74.6 N·m). (i) Do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test, TASK 26-21-00-720-801. (j) Remove the protective caps from each of the discharge outlets. (k) Remove the protective caps from each of the discharge tubes. (l) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, between each gland nut, or between the gland nut and discharge head, depending on the configuration. WARNING USE NITRILE GLOVES FOR SKIN PROTECTION WHEN YOU USE COR- BAN 27L. IF IT GETS ON YOUR SKIN, IMMEDIATELY REMOVE IT WITH WATER. IF THIS MATERIAL GETS IN YOUR EYES, IMMEDIATELY FLUSH YOUR EYES WITH WATER. GET MEDICAL AID. THIS MATERIAL CONTAINS FLAMMABLE AGENTS WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL. (m) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head. 26-21-01 1) The Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location. (n) Connect the tube assembly to the discharge head. NOTE: Yellow is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishing agent to engine number 1. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishing agent to engine number 2. (o) Remove any unwanted compound. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (p) At positions 1 and 3, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 450 ±23 in-lb (50.8 ±2.6 N·m). NOTE: Positions 1 connects T-Fitting to engine fire extinguishing bottle 1. NOTE: Position 3 connects Hose Assembly to engine fire extinguishing bottle 2. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT OF TORQUE. IF YOU APPLY MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT , DAMAGE TO THE FIRE BOTTLE CAN OCCUR. (q) At positions 2 and 4, tighten the nut on the tube assembly to 360 ±18 in-lb (40.7 ±2.0 N·m). NOTE: Positions 2 connects Hose Assembly to engine fire extinguishing bottle 1. NOTE: Position 4 connects T-Fitting to engine fire extinguishing bottle 2. SUBTASK 26-21-01-710-001 (10) Do the following steps before connecting the electrical connector [6] to the squib [7]: WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Before you touch the squib [7], do these tasks for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic discharge: • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802 • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802. SUBTASK 26-21-01-700-001 WARNING MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL. (11) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector 26-21-01 Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector Engine APU Cargo Connector Pins 3 and 4, and 6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2 (a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins. (b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC. (c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes. (d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC. (e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors. (f) Disconnect the multimeter. SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-010 (12) Remove the protective caps [8] or shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, from the squibs [7]. SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-003 (13) Connect the electrical connector [6] to the squib [7]. NOTE: The squib pins can cause damage to the electrical connector if the pins do not correctly engage the electrical connector. SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-002 (14) Test the electrical wiring up to the pressure switch [4], do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Pressure Switch Circuit Test, TASK 26-21-00-730-802. SUBTASK 26-21-01-420-005 (15) Connect the electrical connector [5] to pressure switch [4]. SUBTASK 26-21-01-210-004 (16) Do the visual check of the pressure gauge, do this task: Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check, TASK 26-20-00-210-802.

H. Engine Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation Test

SUBTASK 26-21-01-860-003 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L SUBTASK 26-21-01-741-001 (2) Set the EXT TEST switch, on the P8-1 panel, to the 1 position. (a) Make sure that the L and R lights come on. NOTE: The Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) light will also come on. SUBTASK 26-21-01-741-002 (3) Set the EXT TEST switch, on the P8-1 panel, to the 2 position. (a) Make sure that the L and R lights come on. NOTE: The APU light will also come on. 26-21-01 SUBTASK 26-21-01-741-003 (4) Release the EXT TEST switch.

26-21-02-000-801Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Removal ATA 26 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Removal (Figure 401) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSON AND DAMAGE EQUIPMENT . (1) The squibs are installed on the engine fire extinguishing bottles, located in the left main landing gear wheel well. The removal procedure is the same for each squib, but each squib has a unique location and they are not interchangeable. (2) This task gives instructions to remove the engine fire extinguisher bottle squib.

B. References

Reference Title 20-40-12-000-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description STD-1168 Cap - Shorting or Faraday Cap

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 133 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Left 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

E. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 26-21-02-860-006 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 20 C00297 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS RIGHT B 22 C00296 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS LEFT B 23 C01022 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN R B 24 C01021 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS ALTN L 26-21-02 SUBTASK 26-21-02-860-008 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (2) Before the squib [2] or squib [3] is touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802. F. Engine Fire Extinguisher Bottle Squib Removal SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-001 (1) Disconnect the applicable electrical connector [1] from the squib [2] or squib [3]. NOTE: The squibs are not interchangeable and each squib has a unique location on the discharge port. SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-005 WARNING DO NOT MOVE OR TOUCH THE CARTRIDGE WITHOUT A SHORTING CAP OR FARADAY CAP INSTALLED ON THE ENDS OF THE SQUIB. THE SQUIB IS AN EXPLOSIVE DEVICE. ACCIDENTAL OPERATION OF THE SQUIB CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS. CAUTION DO NOT PUT SHUNT PLUGS ON THE FIRE BOTTLE SQUIBS. THE SHUNT PLUGS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SQUIB PINS. (2) Install the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, on the squib [2] or squib [3]. NOTE: The shorting caps are attached to the fire extinguishing bottles. These shorting caps prevent the squibs from possible activation due to static electricity. SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-006 (3) Remove the lockwire between the squib [2] or squib [3] and discharge outlet. SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-003 (4) Remove the squib [2] or squib [3] from the discharge head. SUBTASK 26-21-02-020-004 (5) Remove and discard the packing [4].

WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE. (d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC. (e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors. SUBTASK 26-21-02-420-002 CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE SQUIB PINS CORRECTLY ENGAGE THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF NOT , DAMAGE TO THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR CAN OCCUR. (4) Connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [2] or squib [3].

G. Prepare for the Installation

SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-004 (1) If the protective caps [6] are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as follows: (a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] is ± 0.10 lb (0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate. 1) Use the force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3]. 2) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as “Value A”. 3) Make sure that protective caps [6] are installed on the squibs [7] and remove the rest of protective caps from fire extinguishing bottle [3], before weighing. NOTE: Only the weight of the protective caps located on the squibs, supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle, are included on the weight listed on the ID plate. 4) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7], protective caps [6] installed on the squibs [7] and record as “Value B”. 5) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] ("Value A") and measured weight ("Value B") is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace the fire extinguishing bottle [3]. SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-005 (2) If the shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168 are installed on the squibs [7], weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as follows: (a) Make sure that the weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] is ± 0.10 lb (0.05 kg) of the weight listed on the bottle data plate 1) Use the force gauge, COM-17114, to weigh the fire extinguishing bottle [3]. 2) Weigh the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, including its caps and record as “Value A”. 3) Record the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] as “Value B”. 4) Remove the remaining protective caps from the fire extinguishing bottle [3] before weighing. 5) Weigh the assembly, including the charged bottle, discharge heads, squibs [7], shorting or faraday caps, STD-1168, and protective caps [6] supplied for the squibs [7] and record as “Value C”. NOTE: Weigh of protective caps supplied with the fire extinguishing bottle for the squibs only are included on ID plate. 6) Find the measured weight of the fire extinguishing bottle [3] by subtracting “Value A” from “Value C” and record as “Value D” (Value D = Value C - Value A). 7) If the difference between the weight marked on the fire extinguishing bottle [3] (“Value B”) and measured weight (“Value D”) is more than 0.10 lb (0.05 kg), replace the fire extinguishing bottle [3]. 26-22-01 SUBTASK 26-22-01-860-002 (3) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 19 C01344 APU FIRE SW POWER B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-4 Row Col Number Name A 14 C00033 AUX POWER UNIT CONT SUBTASK 26-22-01-211-001 (4) If the previous bottle was discharged, check the discharge tube for any debris. SUBTASK 26-22-01-200-001 (5) Do this task: APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Check, TASK 26-20-00-210-801.

H. APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation

SUBTASK 26-22-01-420-001 (1) Do these steps to install the fire extinguishing bottle [3]: WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU MOVE THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE. THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE IS HIGHLY PRESSURIZED AND HAS AN EXPLOSIVE SQUIB AS A COMPONENT . ACCIDENTAL DISCHARGE OF THE FIRE EXTINGUISHING BOTTLE CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Prepare the fire extinguisher bottle lugs and the support bracket mounting surfaces for installation. 1) Remove any old sealant from the bottle lugs and the support bracket. (b) Use sandpaper, G50260, to sand the surface of the support bracket mounting surface until any surface finish is removed. NOTE: Avoid removing surface finish outside of the bonding area. (c) Using a cotton wiper, G00034, and alcohol, B50073, clean the exposed surfaces of the support mounting bracket and the surfaces of the bottle mounting lugs. (d) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new wiper. (e) Apply one of these sealants to the surface of the bottle mounting lugs: • sealant, A50110 • sealant, A50155 • sealant, A50231 • sealant, A50296. (f) Make sure that sealing compound is applied on the full area of the bottle lug surface. NOTE: The thickness of the sealing compound must be 0.0005 in. (0.013 mm). If necessary, the sealing compound thickness can be between 0.010 in. (0.254 mm) and 0.030 in. (0.762 mm) depending on fit-up of parts. (g) Put the fire extinguishing bottle [3] in the mounted position on the support bracket. (h) Install the bolts [4] and washers [5]. 26-22-01 1) Make sure that there is sufficient sealant coming out at each edge of the bottle lugs. 2) Make sure that sealant squeeze out is smooth and approximately 0.06 in. (1.52 mm) along the joint. (i) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the fire extinguishing bottle [3] lugs and support bracket (SWPM 20-20-00). 1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. 2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.0007 ohm (0.7 milliohm) or less. a) If the resistance is more than 0.0007 ohm (0.7 milliohm), remove the fire extinguishing bottle [3] and repeat the previous steps to prepare the bottle surfaces for installation. (j) If the discharge head [11] does not align with the hose assembly [9], or if the lockwire is not installed between the gland nuts [13], do this step: 1) Loosen the discharge outlet gland nut [13] and rotate the discharge port so it aligns with the hose assembly [9]. (k) Torque the gland nut [13] to 540 in-lb (61 N·m) - 660 in-lb (75 N·m). NOTE: Even if the discharge outlet is already aligned, the gland nut may not torqued down. NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the lockwire and torque the gland nut if the gland nut is torqued, and the lockwire installed. (l) Remove protective caps from each of the discharge outlets. (m) Remove protective caps from each of the discharge tubes. (n) Install MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, between the gland nut [13] and discharge outlet boss. WARNING DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (o) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner diameter where the hose assembly [9] attaches to the discharge head [11]. 1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, are also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location. (p) Connect the hose assembly [9] to the discharge head [11]. (q) Remove all unwanted compound. (r) Torque the nut [10] to 342 in-lb (38.6 N·m) - 378 in-lb (42.7 N·m). SUBTASK 26-22-01-710-001 (2) Do these steps to connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [7] on the fire extinguishing bottle [3]: 26-22-01 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE SQUIB BEFORE YOU DO THE PROCEDURES FOR DEVICES THAT ARE SENSITIVE TO ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSON AND DAMAGE EQUIPMENT . (a) Before touching the squib [7], do these tasks for devices that are sensitive to electrostatic discharge: • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802 • ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802. SUBTASK 26-22-01-700-002 WARNING MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. IF THERE IS A VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR, THE SQUIB CAN FIRE SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL. (3) Do these steps to make sure that there is no stray voltage at the electrical squib connector Fire Bottle Squib Electrical Connector Engine APU Cargo Connector Pins 3 and 4, and 6 and 7 3 and 4 1 and 2 (a) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, across the two pins. (b) Make sure that the current is less than 5 milliamps AC, and less than 5 milliamps DC. (c) Connect a resistor, STD-1079, across the multimeter probes. (d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC. (e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors. (f) Disconnect the multimeter. SUBTASK 26-22-01-020-013 (4) Remove protective cap [6] or shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, from the applicable squib [7]. (a) Make sure that the squib [7] that is not used has a protective cap [6] installed. (b) Install the protective cap [6] on the dummy stowage receptacle. SUBTASK 26-22-01-420-003 (5) Connect the electrical connector [1] to the squib [7]. NOTE: The pins of the squib can cause damage to the electrical connector if the pins do not correctly engage. SUBTASK 26-22-01-420-002 (6) Do this task to test the electrical wiring up to the pressure switch [12]: APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Pressure Switch Circuit Test, TASK 26-22-00-730-802. SUBTASK 26-22-01-420-009 (7) Connect the electrical connector [2] to the pressure switch [12]. 26-22-01

I. APU Fire Extinguishing Bottle Installation Test

SUBTASK 26-22-01-860-003 (1) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name B 19 C01344 APU FIRE SW POWER B 21 C00452 FIRE PROTECTION EXTINGUISHERS APU F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-4 Row Col Number Name A 14 C00033 AUX POWER UNIT CONT SUBTASK 26-22-01-741-001 (2) Set the EXT TEST switch, on the P8-1 panel, to the 1 position. (a) Make sure that the L and R lights come on. NOTE: The Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) light will also come on. SUBTASK 26-22-01-741-002 (3) Set the EXT TEST switch, on the P8-1 panel, to the 2 position. (a) Make sure that the L and R lights come on. NOTE: The APU light will also come on. SUBTASK 26-22-01-741-003 (4) Release the EXT TEST switch.

The shorting cap may be used to install on the squib to prevent possibly activation due to static electricity.
