26-23-00-720-803Cargo Fire Dual Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test ATA 26 · Task 720-803

Task Description

8. Cargo Fire Dual Extinguishing Discharge Line Flow Test (Figure 502) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task. 26-23-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued)

A. General

(1) The cargo fire extinguishing discharge line flow test makes sure there is nothing blocking the discharge lines. (2) There are two bottles on the airplane. The contents of bottle 1 is released when the discharge switch is pushed. Bottle 2 is discharged 60 minutes later through a flow filter/dryer. In this test, the discharge lines will be removed from bottle 1, and from the output of the filter/dryers. The flow will be checked from the output of bottle 1 through the discharge nozzles and the filter/dryer output line.

SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-021 (5) Pressurize the fire extinguishing plumbing to 50 psig (345 kPa) to 55 psig (379 kPa). (a) Wait at least 3 minutes for the air pressure to stabilize. SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-022 (6) Turn off the pressure source. (a) Record the stabilized air pressure. (b) Wait 10 minutes, then check the pressure. 1) Make sure the pressure loss during the 10 minute waiting period is not more than 5 psi (34.5 kPa). SUBTASK 26-23-00-780-023 (7) Bleed the fire extinguishing plumbing to 0 psi and disconnect the pressure source. SUBTASK 26-23-00-080-040 (8) Remove the nozzle cap, SPL-1648, from the discharge nozzles in the forward cargo compartment. 26-23-00 RYR 020 POST SB 737-26-1130 (Continued) SUBTASK 26-23-00-480-044 CAUTION DO NOT APPLY AIR PRESSURE TO THE OUTPUT OF THE PRESSURE FILTER/DRIER. 26-23-01-200-803Discharge Head Leak Check (Method 1) ATA 26 · Task 200-803

Task Description

2. Discharge Head Leak Check (Method 1)

A. General

(1) This task gives instructions to do the leak check for the discharge heads of the High Rate Discharge and Low Rate Discharge cargo fire extinguisher bottles. (a) This method is used to connect the air source to the discharge head ports. (2) The discharge head leak check must be done every time the cargo fire extinguisher bottle is installed.

G. Prepare for the Inspection/Check

SUBTASK 26-23-01-860-074 (1) Make sure that the FWD ARM and AFT ARM switches, on the P8 cargo bay fire control panel, are in the unarmed (up) position. NOTE: The ARMED lights will be off to indicate that the system is disarmed. SUBTASK 26-23-01-010-036 (5) If necessary, remove the air conditioning ducts near bottle 2 [2]. SUBTASK 26-23-01-020-059 (6) Disconnect the electrical connector [10] and electrical connectors [11] from the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle (TASK 26-23-01-000-801-001). WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIBS. ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE THE FIRE BOTTLE TO RELEASE ITS CONTENTS SUDDENLY AND CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT (a) Before touching the squibs, do the task for handling devices that are sensitive to electrostatic discharge (TASK 20-40-12-000-802). SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-094 WARNING DO NOT MOVE OR TOUCH THE CARTRIDGE WITHOUT A SHORTING CAP OR FARADAY CAP INSTALLED ON THE ENDS OF THE SQUIB. THE SQUIB IS AN EXPLOSIVE DEVICE. NOTE: These shorting caps prevent the squibs from a possible activation because of static electricity. The two squibs on the fire extinguisher use different size caps.

H. Discharge Head Leak Check

SUBTASK 26-23-01-020-060 (1) Disconnect the discharge flex tubes [12] from the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle discharge ports. SUBTASK 26-23-01-480-006 (2) Connect the 0-60 psig dry filtered regulated air source, STD-18519, or 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, to the discharge head. (a) Use discharge head pressure adapter, COM-25014, or female end pressure adapter, COM-25015, to connect the air supply hose to the FWD or AFT discharge head. (b) If necessary, connect pressure gauge, STD-13740, between the air source and the discharge head. NOTE: It is necessary that the pressure gauge accurately reads a 0.5 psig change. SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-006 (3) Slowly apply pressure 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa) to the discharge head. SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-001 (4) With a Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, make sure that there are no leaks in the air supply hose and the discharge head pressure adapter, COM-25014, or female end pressure adapter, COM-25015. SUBTASK 26-23-01-790-001 (5) Do a check for changes in air pressure. (a) Isolate the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the cargo fire extinguisher bottle and hold 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa). (b) Record the initial pressure value. (c) After a minimum of 5 minutes, record the final pressure value. (d) Make sure that the pressure has not dropped more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa). (e) If the pressure decreases more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa), then do the following: 1) Use Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, to check for leakage in the locations that follow (View C, Reference Not Currently Available): • Around the gland nut • Around the neck of the discharge head • Around the squib 2) Make the necessary adjustments to the discharge head or gland nut to correct the leakage. 3) If necessary, replace the applicable internal O-ring. 4) Repeat steps to check for changes in air pressure. 26-23-01 5) If the pressure does not hold within 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa) of the initial pressure, then replace the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-23-01/401). SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-002 (6) Reduce the dry filtered air or nitrogen source pressure to zero. SUBTASK 26-23-01-080-006 (7) Remove the pressure adapter and the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the fire extinguisher bottle discharge head. SUBTASK 26-23-01-700-014 (8) Repeat the leak check for the remaining discharge heads.

I. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-095 (1) Make sure a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, is installed between the gland nuts. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (2) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred), to the following locations: • Outlets threads of the discharge port • Outlet end of the discharge port • Shoulder sleeve of the discharge flex tube WARNING USE NITRILE GLOVES FOR SKIN PROTECTION WHEN YOU USE COR- BAN 27L. (a) Use Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, as permitted alternative compounds in this location. H. Discharge Head Leak Check

SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-101 (1) Make sure the discharge flex tube [12] are connected to the discharge heads. SUBTASK 26-23-01-020-062 (2) Disconnect the discharge flex tube [12], of the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle, from the tube assembly [13](Figure 601 (Sheet 6)). SUBTASK 26-23-01-480-007 (3) Connect the 0-60 psig dry filtered regulated air source, STD-18519, or 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, to the applicable discharge flex tube [12] positions. (a) Use test equipment, SPL-1647, to connect the air supply hose to the discharge flex tube [12]. (b) If necessary, use reducer, COM-25874, to connect equipment, SPL-1647, to the discharge flex tube [12]. NOTE: The discharge flex tube that connects to the forward squib is smaller. The reducer and SPL1647 both have male connections. The reducer can be attached to SPL1647 with a coupler or a hose with female connections on both ends. SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-003 (4) Slowly apply pressure 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa) to the discharge flex tube. SUBTASK 26-23-01-790-002 (5) With a Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, make sure that there are no leaks in the air supply hose and tubing connections. SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-004 (6) Do a check for changes in air pressure. (a) Isolate the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the cargo fire extinguisher bottle and hold 54 psig (372 kPa) - 56 psig (386 kPa). (b) Record the initial pressure value. (c) After a minimum of 5 minutes, record the final pressure value. (d) Make sure that the pressure has not dropped more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa). (e) If the pressure decreases more than 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa), then do the following: 1) Use Snoop Leak Detector compound, G00091, to check for leakage in the locations that follow: • Around the gland nut • Around the neck of the discharge head • Around the squib 2) Make the necessary adjustments to the discharge head or gland nut to correct the leakage. 3) If necessary, replace the applicable internal O-ring. 4) Repeat steps to check for changes in air pressure. 5) If the pressure does not hold within 0.5 psig (3.4 kPa) of the initial pressure, then replace the applicable cargo fire extinguisher bottle (PAGEBLOCK 26-23-01/401). SUBTASK 26-23-01-780-005 (7) Reduce the dry filtered air or nitrogen source pressure to zero. 26-23-01 SUBTASK 26-23-01-080-007 (8) Remove the test equipment, SPL-1647, and the dry filtered air or nitrogen source from the discharge flex tube [12]. SUBTASK 26-23-01-700-015 (9) Repeat the leak check for the remaining discharge heads.

(a) Use Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136, as permitted alternative compounds in this location. SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-103 (3) Connect the FWD (Yellow) and AFT (Blue) discharge flex tubes [12] to the tube assemblies [13]. (a) Refer to the outlet identification above each discharge outlet to make sure that the discharge flex tubes [12]are connected to the correct tube assemblies [13]. SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-104 (4) Tighten the nuts on the discharge flex tubes [12]to these torque values: Discharge Tube Torque Values Nut Size Tube Outer Diameter Torque (MIN) Torque (MAX) 7/8 in 1/2 in 266 in-lb (30 N·m) 294 in-lb (33 N·m) 1 in 5/8 in 342 in-lb (39 N·m) 378 in-lb (43 N·m) SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-105 (5) Install the electrical connector [10] to the pressure switch on bottle 1 [1] or bottle 2 [2] (TASK 26-23-01-400-802-001). 26-23-01 SUBTASK 26-23-01-500-002 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE PINS ON THE SQUIBS. (d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC. (e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors. (f) Disconnect the multimeter. SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-106 (8) Install the electrical connectors [11] to the squibs on bottle 1 [1] or bottle 2 [2] (TASK 26-23-01-400-802-001). (b) Remove the shorting or faraday cap, STD-1168, from the squibs. (c) Make sure that the squib pins are not bent or damaged. SUBTASK 26-23-01-420-011 (1) Connect the discharge tube to the discharge outlet on the bottle. WARNING DO NOT GET THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUND ON YOUR SKIN, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. THE COMPOUND IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. USE APPROVED GLOVES, AND EYE PROTECTION. MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS GOOD AIRFLOW IN THE WORK AREA. KEEP THE COMPOUND AWAY FROM SPARKS AND FLAMES. THE COMPOUND CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Apply a layer of compound, D50004 (preferred) to the outlet threads, outlet end, and outlet inner diameter where the tube assembly attaches to the discharge head. 26-23-01 1) Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237 or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136 are also acceptable alternatives as anti-corrosion compounds to use in this location. Blue is used to show the plumbing which supplies extinguishant to cargo compartment number 2. (c) Remove any unwanted compound. Make sure the holes in the discharge nozzle [3] and spacer [2] line up with those in the pan cover assembly. (b) Tighten the nut 360 ±18 in-lb (41 ±2 N·m). (c) Secure the discharge nozzle [3] with screws [4]. (d) Install the cargo ceiling. (e) Position the spacer [1] and retainer [5], and secure with screws [6]. (d) Install a new o-ring [3] on the discharge cartridge (squib) [1]. (e) Install the discharge cartridge (squib) [1] in the discharge head. (f) Tighten the discharge cartridge (squib) [1] to 90 in-lb (10 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11 N·m). SUBTASK 26-23-02-700-001 WARNING MAKE SURE THERE IS NO VOLTAGE AT THE ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR. (d) Make sure that the voltage is less than 0.250 volts AC, and less than 0.250 volts DC. (e) If it is necessary, repeat the steps above for the other squib electrical connectors. (f) Disconnect the multimeter. SUBTASK 26-23-02-420-006 (3) Make sure that the electrical connector is not damaged. NOTE: The squib pins can cause damage to the electrical connector if the pins do not correctly engage with the electrical connector. Halon Fire Extinguishers Inspection SUBTASK 26-26-01-210-001 (1) Make sure that the applicable decals, nameplates, and labels are in good condition. 26-26-02-200-801Water Fire Extinguishers - Inspection/Check ATA 26 · Task 200-801

Task Description

2. Water Fire Extinguishers - Inspection/Check (Figure 601)

A. General

(1) The portable water fire extinguishers are installed in several general locations in the passenger compartment. If the water fire extinguisher is not easy to see, a placard will identify the location. The water fire extinguishers are usually installed in one or more of these areas: galley or lavatory stowage areas, closets, doghouses, near attendant seats. (2) This task has a weight check of the portable water fire extinguisher assembly and the weight check of the CO 2 cartridge.

(a) Use a force gauge, COM-11525, push/pull scale 0-25 pound (0-11 kilogram), STD-753, or equivalent to make sure that the weight of the water fire extinguisher is not less than the weight shown on the extinguisher nameplate. SUBTASK 26-26-02-210-019 (8) Make sure that the water fire extinguisher is not pressurized. WARNING BEFORE YOU DISASSEMBLE THE EXTINGUISHER, MAKE SURE THAT YOU REMOVE ALL THE PRESSURE FROM THE FIRE EXTINGUISHER. WHEN YOU DISASSEMBLE A PRESSURIZED FIRE EXTINGUISHER, IT CAN CAUSE INJURY . (a) Do a visual check of the fire extinguisher. 1) Make sure that the lockwire seal [1] (seal wire, G51248) is not broken or missing. 2) Make sure that the red line on the valve assembly and the red line on the cartridge holder [5] are aligned. (b) If the lockwire seal [1] (seal wire, G51248) is broken, missing, or the cartridge holder [5] alignment is not correct, do these steps: 1) Push and hold the valve assembly lever down. a) If there is air pressure and/or extinguishing agent released at the nozzle, replace the water fire extinguisher. SUBTASK 26-26-02-020-005 (9) Remove the CO 2 cartridge [2]. (a) Remove and discard the lockwire seal [1]. (b) Turn the cartridge holder [5] counterclockwise and remove it from the valve assembly. (c) Remove the spring [4] and CO 2 cartridge [2] from the cartridge holder [5]. 1) Discard the spring [4]. 2) If the CO 2 cartridge [2] is damaged or used, replace the CO 2 cartridge [2]. (d) Remove the O-ring [3] from the valve assembly. 1) Discard the O-ring [3]. SUBTASK 26-26-02-210-020 (10) Do a weight check of the CO 2 cartridge [2]. (a) Put the CO 2 cartridge [2] on a scale (2 lb force gauge, COM-16140, or equivalent). (b) Make sure that the CO 2 cartridge [2] weighs a minimum of 0.073 lb (33 g). 1) If the CO 2 cartridge [2] weighs less than 0.073 lb (33 g), discard the CO 2 cartridge [2] and install a new CO 2 cartridge [2]. SUBTASK 26-26-02-420-004 (11) Assemble the cartridge holder [5]. (a) Put a thin layer of lubricant, D00062, on the new O-ring [3]. 26-26-02 (b) Install the O-ring [3] in the valve assembly. (c) Put a thin layer of grease, D00013, on the internal threads of the cartridge holder [5]. (d) Install the new spring [4] and CO 2 cartridge [2] in the cartridge holder [5]. (e) Install the cartridge holder [5] in the valve assembly and turn the cartridge holder [5] clockwise until: 1) The red line on the cartridge holder [5] aligns with the red line on the valve assembly. 2) The lockwire seal [1] holes in the lock ring [6] and valve assembly are aligned. a) If it is necessary, loosen the setscrews [7] to adjust the position of the lock ring [6]. (f) Install a new lockwire seal [1] (seal wire, G51248) through the holes in the lock ring [6] and the body of the valve assembly. (g) Install the wires through the lead seal. 1) Pull the wires tight and crimp the lead seal with the crimping tool, STD-252. NOTE: It is satisfactory to use a standard plier to crimp the lead seal. SUBTASK 26-26-02-210-002 (12) Make sure that the mounting bracket is attached correctly to the airplane. SUBTASK 26-26-02-210-012 (13) Do this task: Water Fire Extinguisher Installation, TASK 26-26-02-400-801. SUBTASK 26-26-02-210-003 (14) Make sure that the water fire extinguisher is installed tightly to the mounting bracket.

WARNING BEFORE YOU DISASSEMBLE THE EXTINGUISHER, MAKE SURE THAT YOU REMOVE ALL THE PRESSURE FROM THE FIRE EXTINGUISHER. WHEN YOU DISASSEMBLE A PRESSURIZED FIRE EXTINGUISHER, IT CAN CAUSE INJURY .
